amended-evidence: 2026-09-19 RECALLED, source body inlined 2026-09-12 verbatim from BACKLOG.md:77-87 before that file's deletion: 'READY 2026-08-26 — dev-notes/ needs its OBSERVATIONS carrier. Design, decided: copy dispatch-guards four-slot form (incident + basis, class, pre-formulated rule text, consumer + drain seam) into dev-notes/lifecycle-OBSERVATIONS.md, same-class entries merging into the existing entry rather than a sibling; provenance dev-notes/OBSERVATIONS-FORM.md in that repo.' MEASURED at this desk 2026-09-19: dev-notes/ holds exactly one file, README.md, so no OBSERVATIONS carrier exists here and this entry is still open. MEASURED, same read: that README states the carrier class is registered as the maintenance notes kind and that lc-78 asks whether it becomes its own registered kind, so the ordering this entry already names is confirmed at the artifact rather than recalled. THE FIRST ENTRY IS NOW WRITTEN AND WAITING, so this entry's builder pastes rather than composes. ENTRY ONE, four slots. INCIDENT plus BASIS, RELAYED from the lc-224 act 1 lane and carried as theirs, not reproduced here: writing 18 red-first tests against the unchanged declaration.py, it found FIVE passing for the WRONG REASON, because the old code rejected any non-string reader entry with one coarse declaration_malformed, so every finer sub-rule the change introduced (a when on a prefixed reader, an unknown when mode, a none with no reason, a predicate naming nothing, an object missing its reader key) produced that same ident on old code; an ident-only assertion therefore went red on old code and green on new code while never exercising the rule it was written for. RELAYED: the lane caught this mid-build, upgraded those five to message-content assertions and re-ran before trusting the arrangement. MEASURED at this desk, the part I did verify: the shipped suite is 779 pass 0 fail 0 skip and lifecycle --test is 113 rows CLEAN, so whatever the arrangement was, the landed state is green; I did NOT re-run the lane's red-first and do not claim it. DERIVED, class: instrument discrimination at the coarse-to-fine seam. A red-first proof is only as discriminating as the thing it asserts, and a refusal IDENT is the coarsest thing available, so where a validator already rejects a whole CATEGORY every finer rule inside it inherits the category's ident and the proof becomes could-not-verify wearing a red. DERIVED, pre-formulated rule text ready to apply: when a validator is extended from a coarse shape check to a finer rule INSIDE the shape it already rejects, the red-first assertion pins MESSAGE CONTENT or the code path and never the refusal ident alone, because the old code must be unable to produce the asserted red for ANY reason rather than merely unable to pass. DERIVED, consumer plus drain seam: the consumer is the next session extending a declaration validator; the drain seam is this carrier's retirement pass, which applies the text or discards it with a one-line reason. ENTRY TWO, kept separate because it is a different class. RELAYED from the same lane: _needs_why requires a reason of at least 8 characters and a fixture reason of 'fixture' is 7, so a control test went red for a reason unrelated to the feature under test. DERIVED: what mattered is that the lane read WHY the control was red instead of accepting it, which is the control doing its job. DERIVED, candidate text: a fixture reason passed to a declared-why slot is at least 8 characters, and a control that goes red is read for its reason before the arrangement is trusted. NOT MEASURED and deliberately not claimed: _needs_why's threshold was not read at the source by this desk, so the 8-character figure is the lane's and is carried as theirs.

Measured: audit's use-evidence render is judgment.py:215-235 and is per-RULE only; a grep for lane across the whole audit output returns seven hits, none a use-evidence column. This entry's done-criterion emits a NEW finding — 'kind check reports a declaration listing the reserved tend as a redundant-declaration finding (red-first)' — and its write set named cli.py and declaration.py only. Law 2 makes every refusal a registry row with its firing input; a refusal's realizing file is always refusals.py; so the boundary as written commissions work that cannot be finished inside it. goal: one-home-per-kind

Positive control first: kind sweep on the untouched tree reports FINDING [unregistered_persisted_thing] 1 tracked file, plugin/workflows/.gitkeep. Then a root TRACKING.md was planted and committed: the same sweep reports 2 tracked files and names TRACKING.md, so the stray .md case is ALREADY covered and only the placement and the fourth disposition are missing. The absence is shown rather than assumed: lifecycle audit over that same planted tree mentions neither TRACKING.md nor any stray, its output being the 19 kind sections, the walk summary line and the judgment register, while the sweep on the identical tree names the file.
blocked-by: decision whether a DECLARED CUSTOM with a named consumer becomes a fourth disposition beside the sweep's three, or whether every tracked file must resolve to a registered kind with no fourth and the CLAUDE.md consumer note is documentation rather than a disposition
amend-reason: 2026-09-12 2026-09-12 desk: fold the operator's machine-read exception registry, the tool-owned declaration class and its ownership-succession slot into the entry text, per the judgment desk's three design inputs
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-12 lifecycle audit surfaces every tracked file that resolves to no registered kind, red-first against a planted stray tracking .md which the audit walk today never mentions while kind sweep names it in the same tree; AND the disposition vocabulary either gains the declared-custom fourth, which names its consumer in the repo's CLAUDE.md and is checked there rather than trusted, or the sweep's own text states why there is no fourth. MUST-NOT-MOVE: kind sweep keeps its current finding text and exit code, and a tree with no stray still reports CLEAN rather than silence. DESIGN CONSTRAINT (operator, first-hand, binding): kind admission means SHAPE REDESIGN — slot-structured entries a checker can anchor on POSITIONALLY, a closed grade vocabulary, explicit drain semantics, never a blind carry-over of free prose; carried-over content is marked LEGACY. EXCEPTION REGISTRY (operator, first-hand): the sweep's exception registry is MACHINE-READ from the repo's .claude/lifecycle.json (declared customs with named consumer, exempt globs); CLAUDE.md carries only the human-facing rationale pointing at it. The sweep anchors on the DECLARATION FILE, never on prose — a word-presence check over free prose both over-fires and under-fires. TOOL-OWNED CLASS (operator, first-hand): a declaration class 'tool-owned: <glob> -> <owning tool>[, succeeded by <tool>]' in lifecycle.json. Boundary is WHO WRITES: files a tool writes programmatically in its own format for its own consumption (run logs, fire logs, replay state) sit OUTSIDE the carrier machinery — no grades, no ratio, no retire walk — and INSIDE the accounting, so the sweep counts them accounted and 'tool state' never becomes an undeclared exemption. Retention and rotation policy live in the OWNING tool's repo, never the host repo. OWNERSHIP SUCCESSION is expressible: current owner plus optional successor, because a tool-owned path does not have one owner forever. Repo-residence must be justified in the entry: shared fixtures yes, caches and scratch to XDG data/state per existing doctrine; the gitignored variant is covered by promote-or-declare-local with the owner named.

## lc-78
grade: PARKED
requirement: dev-notes/*-OBSERVATIONS.md carriers are capture-and-drain queues (four slots: incident plus basis, class, pre-formulated rule text, consumer plus drain seam) that are drained only by DOCTRINE today: no kind registers them, so they sit outside the flow and ratio machinery and outside the retire walk, and nothing detects a carrier that captures without draining. That is the assumed-delivery shape the doctrine itself names: a step with no named actor does not fail, it ACCUMULATES. record: retirement pass R1 dispatch, 2026-09-12
goal: one-home-per-kind
write-set: .claude/lifecycle.json,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,test/test_declaration.py,dev-notes/lifecycle-OBSERVATIONS.md
done-criterion: an OBSERVATIONS kind is registered with all six stages, so the carrier appears in the retire walk and its capture-against-drain reads like the item carrier's. THE SHAPE IS REDESIGNED, NOT CARRIED OVER (operator, first-hand, binding): entries become slot-structured so a checker anchors POSITIONALLY, the grade vocabulary is closed, and drain semantics are explicit. OPEN AND CLOSED SPLIT, named as part of the design rather than left to the migration: APPLIED and otherwise closed entries are HISTORY and go to a legacy segment or the done home marked LEGACY and not shape-checked, while OPEN entries re-book into the new shape, so only the queue migrates. Red-first on the real fill measured below, not a fixture. MUST-NOT-MOVE: dev-notes/ AS A DIRECTORY keeps its role and home, since sitting outside every operational load path is load-bearing separation; the kind covers the files within it, never the directory's placement.
evidence: CURRENT FILL, measured 2026-09-12. In THIS repo: dev-notes/ holds exactly one file, README.md, 3 lines, and no OBSERVATIONS carrier exists at all, which is what lc-8 books creating. In dotfiles: dev-notes/hooks-OBSERVATIONS.md is 429 lines (wc -l, the dispatcher's census re-run here) carrying 3 entries under ## headings. THE SHAPE ARGUMENT IS MEASURED IN THAT FILE: its status words sit in free prose rather than in a slot, so a scan for them returns 9 lowercase 'closed', 4 lowercase 'applied', 2 'OPEN', 2 'APPLIED', 2 'pending', 1 lowercase 'open', 1 'discarded' across 3 entries. A word-presence checker over that text fires on entries that merely DISCUSS a grade and is silenced by ordinary sentences carrying one, which is exactly why the doctrine says a carrier checker anchors on the slot's position and never on a word occurring anywhere.

## lc-79
grade: STANDBY
requirement: There is no GROWTH-EXEMPT class, so closed history and unfinished machinery read alike. The growth vocabulary is three values (bounded-by-exit, compacted, unbounded-with-reason) and a kind whose declared exit action this build does not perform is printed as 'growth unchecked', which the walk summary counts: 8 kinds today. That one bucket holds two different facts, a kind whose exit is genuinely unimplemented and a kind that is CLOSED HISTORY with nothing to drain, and a reader cannot tell them apart. The journal, the design directives and the done-home archive are the second sort. record: retirement pass R1 dispatch, 2026-09-12
goal: one-home-per-kind
write-set: .claude/lifecycle.json,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/retire.py,test/test_declaration.py
done-criterion: journal entries, the design directives and the done-home archive are each registered as kinds carrying an explicit growth-exempt class: covered by the stray sweep so nothing about them is unregistered, and exempt from the flow alarm with the exemption DECLARED and its reason stated, because closed history has nothing to drain. Red-first: a growth-exempt kind must be visibly distinguishable in the retire walk from a kind counted 'growth unchecked' because its exit is unimplemented, so the proof is the walk summary separating today's single bucket of 8 into the two facts it currently conflates. MUST-NOT-MOVE: no kind gains an exemption by default, an undeclared stage stays a finding (invariant 2), and growth stays controlled by FLOW rather than size (R22), the exemption being a declared property of closed history and never a cap. DESIGN CONSTRAINT (operator, first-hand, binding): kind admission means SHAPE REDESIGN, so any of these carriers gaining a kind gets slot structure a checker anchors on POSITIONALLY and a closed vocabulary, with content that must carry over as-is marked LEGACY, which the done-home archive already models: it is held verbatim and not shape-checked.
evidence: MEASURED 2026-09-12 at 2c77eee. The declaration's growth values are exactly three: bounded-by-exit, compacted, unbounded-with-reason, read from .claude/lifecycle.json's 19 kinds. lifecycle retire prints per kind 'growth check: NOT CHECKED, the declared exit action X is not one this build performs (move), so there is no recorded event to look for. NOT the same answer as its exit never fired', and the walk summary line reads 'walk: 19 kind(s); grew-without-exit 1 (items); growth unchecked 8 (refusal-registry, git hooks, plugin manifest, marketplace manifest, maintenance notes, git config, plugin cache versions, legacy backlog)'. POPULATION NOTE, so the entry is not read wider than it is: journal entries IS registered here (home JOURNAL.md, growth unbounded-with-reason) and the done-home archive is inside the done-bodies kind (compacted, archive held verbatim), while docs/directives has NO home in this repo at all, the only docs kind being audits at docs/audits/*.md and docs/ holding exactly one file. So this entry adds a CLASS to the vocabulary and re-declares three populations under it, rather than registering three absent kinds.
blocked-by: decision whether growth-exempt is a FOURTH value in the growth vocabulary or a separate declared flag beside the existing three, and whether the exemption is per kind or per segment (the done-home archive is a segment inside a kind, not a kind)
amend-reason: 2026-09-13 Drain desk 2026-09-13, before this item's lane opened. The blocker is discharged: the operator answered both halves of the question this afternoon, and an entry still carrying that blocker would either send a lane to a settled question or invite it to settle a vocabulary decision at the executing tier — in a declaration this repo's other checks read. Recorded here rather than left to the brief so the ANSWER lives with the entry and survives this desk. The stale 8-kinds figure is marked unverified in the same pass, because lc-61 changed the kind population hours ago and a red-first keyed to a carried number is the anchor defect this repo books against.
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-13 ANSWERED, operator decision 2026-09-13 (LEDGER.md:77, all-as-recommended via the judgment desk): growth-exempt is a FOURTH VALUE in the growth vocabulary, named closed-history, declared PER KIND, with a SEGMENT QUALIFIER the owning kind declares for the done-home-archive case. NO separate flag beside the existing three. So: the growth vocabulary becomes four values (bounded-by-exit, compacted, unbounded-with-reason, closed-history); journal entries, the design directives and the done-home archive each carry their growth class explicitly, covered by the stray sweep so nothing about them is unregistered, and exempt from the FLOW alarm with the exemption declared and its reason stated, because closed history has nothing to drain. The done-home archive is a SEGMENT inside a kind rather than a kind, and the owning kind declares that qualifier. RED-FIRST: a closed-history kind must be VISIBLY DISTINGUISHABLE from a kind whose exit this build merely does not perform — today both print as 'growth unchecked' and the walk summary counts them in one bucket, so the red is that the two facts are indistinguishable in the output. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the three existing growth values keep their exact semantics and every kind currently declaring one keeps its current classification; kind check stays CLEAN; and the flow alarm's arithmetic over NON-exempt kinds is unchanged, because an alarm that moves when an exemption is added would be measuring the exemption rather than the flow.
amended-evidence: 2026-09-13 DECISION OPENED AT THE ARTIFACT, not taken from a relay: LEDGER.md:77 in this repo carries it as an operator decision dated 2026-09-13, all-as-recommended via the judgment desk, and it answers both halves this entry's blocker asked — fourth-value-not-flag, and per-kind-with-a-segment-qualifier. THE POPULATION THIS ENTRY CITES HAS MOVED, and the mover was this desk: the entry says the walk summary counts 8 kinds as 'growth unchecked', measured before lc-61 landed at 13dd4bb and registered two NEW kinds (directives, workflow definitions), taking the declaration from 19 kinds to 21. Whether those two land in the unchecked bucket is UNVERIFIED at this amendment — the number 8 is carried, not re-measured, and the build re-derives it from the walk rather than trusting it. Named as unverified rather than re-run because the count is the build's own red-first input and re-measuring it here would put the desk's number where the lane's measurement belongs.

amended-evidence: 2026-09-13 WRITE-SET CORRECTED 2026-09-13 BY A LANE'S REFUSAL, then verified at the code by the drain desk rather than relayed. A dispatched terra lane halted before its first edit on a STOP signal the brief itself set (report a growth-vocabulary consumer outside the owned files) and returned the question with evidence. CONSUMERS CONFIRMED AT e05e29f by grep over plugin/, tools/ and test/: GROWTH_MODES is defined at declaration.py:84 and used there at :190, :910, :913, :916 (all inside the old write-set), but ALSO at migrate.py:1817 ('for mode in decl.GROWTH_MODES:') and migrate.py:1833, neither of which the slot named. refusals.py additionally embeds growth strings as roster FIXTURES at :63, :641, :651, :659 and :676, so a fourth value has a roster surface too. THE CLASS: this is the realization-surface rule, not a typo. A vocabulary addition's write-set is complete only once every CONSUMER of that vocabulary is resolved to a file, and a slot naming only the declaration plus its own validator reads complete while omitting the loop that iterates the tuple. Same shape as lc-33's and lc-68's roster-row omission earlier this wave, one layer out: there the missing surface was the refusal registry, here it is the vocabulary's consumers. NOT YET VERIFIED, and left for the build: whether refusals.py needs a NEW fixture for the fourth value or merely tolerates it, and whether migrate.py:1817's loop is correct unchanged once the tuple grows.

## lc-93
grade: PARKED
requirement: the session-start banner is plugin-worthy: every surfacing a fresh session gets on the operator's machine (item-carrier check, ready head, retirement-ratio line, ledger tail) is wired through machine-local settings.json hooks even though the tools it runs ship WITH this plugin — so an external install gets a carrier with no surfacing, and sessions start blind (statiker desk 1b204567's replication read, 2026-09-13). The fix is a plugin-shipped SessionStart hook: where the cwd repo carries .claude/lifecycle.json it prints item check + ready --head + the ratio line + the LEDGER.md tail; where it does not, it prints nothing (repo-scoped, zero noise elsewhere)

grade: STANDBY
requirement: This repo's own documented verify command does not work. CLAUDE.md '## Verify' names 'python3 -m unittest discover -s test -p test_*.py -t .', and '-t .' puts the repo ROOT on sys.path and never test/, so test_migrate_residue (imports test_migrate) and test_tend_goal (imports test_init) die at import: the documented invocation reports Ran 306, FAILED (errors=2) while the honest suite is Ran 340, OK. A verify line that reports two errors on a clean tree is a check firing on a non-defect - it trains the reader to discount the red, which is the reflex that kills a suite the day its red is real - record: lc-92 lane report slot (b), 2026-09-13
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: CLAUDE.md,test/

Stated as the OUTCOME because either shape satisfies it and the site list does not carry its own completeness check. MUST-NOT-MOVE: discovery still reaches ALL the tests - a command that goes green by finding 306 instead of 340 is the defect with the sign flipped, so the count is asserted, not just the exit status
evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK 2026-09-13, both invocations run on a clean tree at b260152: with '-t .' Ran 306, FAILED (errors=2); without it, Ran 340, OK. amended-requirement: 2026-09-13 This repo's own '## Verify' section misdescribes its own checks, in THREE places, all one class: a verify section whose commands and numbers were never re-derived from the things they describe. (i) THE COMMAND DOES NOT WORK: CLAUDE.md:289 names 'python3 -m unittest discover -s test -p test_*.py -t .' and '-t .' makes the repo root the top level, so the two modules that import siblings by bare name (test_migrate_residue:34, test_tend_goal:38) fail to import - the declared command reports Ran 334, FAILED (errors=2) where the working invocation reports Ran 368, OK. So 36 tests never execute UNDER THE DECLARED COMMAND - they are not uncovered, and the difference matters because a coverage hole is a lane while a wrong command is one pass. The imports are written for a top-level-dir of test/, which is what discover uses when -t is omitted. ## lc-96
grade: PARKED
requirement: Retiring a source carrier needs a RE-ROOTING verb, and lc-86's refusal names the gap without filling it. lc-86 makes --retire-source refuse while any anchor in the successor homes pointing at the carrier under retirement lacks its 'at blob <sha>' pin - correct, because deleting the source breaks EARLIER migrations' unpinned anchors, which is exactly what dotfiles avoided by re-rooting 403 citations before it deleted its two carriers. But the tool ships no way to DO that re-rooting: a repo whose earlier runs wrote bare '<path>:<line>' anchors hits a refusal it can only satisfy by hand-editing a carrier the tool declares itself the only writer of (law 8) - record: lc-86 build lane critique C3, 2026-09-13
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,test/test_migrate.py
done-criterion: a repo holding pre-change bare '<path>:<line>' and '<path>:<line>-<end>' anchors can re-root them to the family form 'at blob <sha>' through the tool, against a stated blob, without hand-editing a carrier - and --retire-source's refusal then passes on the same repo. MUST-NOT-MOVE, and it is the whole risk: re-rooting rewrites carrier bodies, so an anchor whose line range does NOT resolve in the named blob is refused and reported rather than pinned to a blob that does not contain it - the confident-wrong pointer dotfiles measured at 313 of 318 in another repo. The pass is idempotent: re-rooting an already-pinned anchor changes nothing
evidence: SURFACED by the lc-86 build lane 2026-09-13 as the consequence of ruling C3 at this desk: the widened refusal is the right shape - refusing loudly beats deleting silently - and it names a state the tool cannot itself repair. The precedent that makes the gap concrete is dotfiles' own retirement, where citations were re-rooted in ab067d6 BEFORE the carriers were deleted, by hand, in a repo that had no verb for it either

amend-reason: 2026-09-13 blocker DISCHARGED, not dropped: lc-96 was blocked on lc-86 because the refusal this verb must satisfy did not exist yet and its final shape would fix the anchor forms the verb has to recognise. lc-86 closed today at f8878e0/2d70f71/381ce1c and both are now settled: the refusal is 'retire_source_unpinned_anchor', registered and red-proven, and the anchor forms are exactly two - the bare '<path>:<line>' and '<path>:<line>-<end>' that build_items wrote before the pin, against the pinned form '<path>:<line> at blob <sha>' it writes now, with the shipped detector carrying a '(?![\d-])' lookahead so a PINNED anchor is not read as unpinned. That was the named missing evidence and it is in hand

grade: STANDBY
requirement: a retire makes the migrated repo's own audit go from CLEAN to FINDING, and nothing decides whether that is right. lc-86's --retire-source writes the deletion record into the declared laws file; retire.laws_scope_audit then reports 'FINDING [laws_scope_audit] 2 line(s) POSSIBLY MIS-HOMED' against that record's date and its file-shaped tokens. Measured on the real artifact by the lc-86 build lane, not reasoned. It is a review finding and never a refusal, so nothing breaks - but every repo that retires a carrier acquires a standing audit finding it cannot clear, and a finding nobody can clear is the discount reflex again: the next real laws_scope_audit hit in that repo reads as the known one - record: lc-86 build lane report 5/6, 2026-09-13

## lc-104
grade: STANDBY
requirement: wrong-repo invocations under cwd resolution: an ident-taking verb run in the wrong repo fails only by LUCK (unknown_item — the ident happens not to exist there), and prefix-less verbs (ledger add, retire) land SILENTLY in whichever repo cwd points at. Two mechanisms: (1) every ident-taking verb checks the ident's prefix against the repo's declared id-prefix BEFORE existence, refusing with the diagnosis 'ident prefix X does not match this repo's prefix Y — wrong repo? pass --repo'; (2) every verb's first output line names the resolved repo path, so the silent class is visible at the artifact. A harness-side hook was considered and declined: cwd INTENT is not computable outside the CLI, so a hook over-fires on legitimate cwd-resolved use — the prefix predicate inside the CLI is computable with near-zero false fires

grade: STANDBY
requirement: AN EXPLICIT-ZERO ASSERTION IS SATISFIED BY A DEAD PRODUCER, so it is never the arm that proves the producer runs — and this repo applies explicit-zero discipline WIDELY, because law 1 requires a stated zero rather than an omitted key. Measured instance, lc-124's battery: the arm asserting 'SERIALIZE: 0 — none' over a no-crossing fixture stayed GREEN under a mutation that dropped EVERY warning, because a fixture with no crossings legitimately expects that line whether the producer runs or not. Unknown: how many other explicit-zero assertions in this repo's batteries have the same shape — an arm that would stay green if the thing it reports on stopped being computed — record: lc-124 closing report slot (e) lesson 2, 2026-09-15, found by the lane in its own battery rather than shipped

The sweep's own output names every explicit-zero arm found and its disposition, so a zero count here is itself a stated zero rather than an omitted key

## lc-139
grade: STANDBY
requirement: item waves computes its join over WRITE sets only, so a lane that edits a shared VERIFICATION INSTRUMENT collides with every lane that RUNS that instrument and the join reports the two as disjoint. The verb prints that the lanes are disjoint by construction and that the whole set of lanes is the PARALLEL set — an assurance wider than its predicate establishes, which is what stops anyone looking. Record: drain wave 1 composition, 2026-09-15, desk dotfiles-f1.
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/cli.py,test/test_waves.py
done-criterion: The join either REPORTS an instrument-read collision or its own output states, in the verb text, that its predicate covers write-write only and names what it therefore cannot see. Red-first on the measured wave-1 case: tools/prove-rows.py in one lane write-set while three sibling lanes run it as their verifier — today the verb reports 1 lane over 47 path-valued items and no collision, and the GROUPS cut places the prove-rows group beside groups whose verification executes it. An assertion FAILURE at the defect, not an error. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the honest COULD NOT VERIFY line for items outside the lanes keeps its exact text, and no item gains a phantom collision — the discriminating pair is an instrument-read collision that IS reported beside an ordinary disjoint pair that is NOT.
evidence: Measured while composing drain wave 1 at 2387394, desk dotfiles-f1: item waves --grouped put tools/prove-rows.py in the lane-B write set and placed the items.py, init.py, cli.py and test-file groups in the parallel set beside it, reporting no collision between them — while this repo declared Verify section makes python3 tools/prove-rows.py a verifier every one of those lanes runs. The desk had to serialize by hand and mandate a private clone per lane. Sharpened by lc-133: prove-rows mutates core files in whatever tree it runs in and restores at the end of each arm, so the unseen collision is not merely a stale read but a live mutation in a co-writer tree. Class named in the dispatch skill escalation ladder (overlap counts any agent READ-OR-EXECUTE set against another write set, not only write against write) — opened at that text, 2026-09-15; what is novel here is that this repo join cannot express it. ## lc-140
grade: STANDBY
requirement: Invariant 5 says every exit is recorded with its reason and its commit, but the done-home check does not enforce that for a DONE body: two bodies carry NEITHER closed-reason NOR closed-ref and item check reports done-home CLEAN over them. A closure with no ref cannot be resolved to a change at all, so the exit record the doctrine calls load-bearing is simply absent and nothing says so. Record: desk measurement during drain wave 1, 2026-09-15.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: The done-home check answers on a DONE body missing its closure record, and the answer is DECIDED rather than defaulted: either a FINDING, or an explicitly declared historical exemption the check itself verifies by a stated boundary. Red-first on the two real bodies: today item check exits 0 with done-home CLEAN over lc-15 and lc-64; the new check reports them, as an assertion FAILURE at the defect, not an error. MUST-NOT-MOVE, the over-fire arm that decides shippability: a DROPPED body legitimately carries no closure lines — the lc-44 ruling keeps a drop reason in the LEDGER — and all 18 DROPPED bodies in the done home today must stay silent under the new check. A check that fires on those has re-described the lc-44 ruling as a defect. The discriminating pair is therefore one DONE body without closure lines that FIRES beside one DROPPED body without closure lines that does NOT.
evidence: Executed at the desk over the REAL carrier 2026-09-15 at 11dc379, not a fixture: 67 done blocks parsed; crossed against grade, DONE-with-closure-lines 47, DONE-with-NONE 2 (lc-15 at ITEMS-DONE.md line 8, lc-64 at line 82 — both well above the Archive section at line 821, so neither is archive material held verbatim), DROPPED-with-NONE 18, DROPPED-with-closure-lines 0. The DROPPED column is the lc-44 ruling holding perfectly, which is what makes the DONE column a defect rather than a convention. item check over the same carrier in the same state printed done-home check: CLEAN — 0 finding(s). OPEN DESIGN QUESTION, named so it is not re-derived and NOT settled here: lc-15 and lc-64 may predate the close verb writing those slots, in which case the choice is backfill from their closing commits or a declared historical boundary in the check — deciding it needs their closure history read, which this booking does not do.

## lc-147
grade: STANDBY
requirement: THE LEAK SCANNER'S OWN BATTERY GIVES A DIFFERENT ANSWER IN A CLONE THAN IN THE REAL CHECKOUT, and this repo's whole build discipline runs batteries in private clones — so every lane meets phantom failures and learns to discount a red in the one battery that guards law 7. Two of its tests are LOCATION-sensitive: 'foreign-path: a path under THIS REPO's own root does not fire' and 'foreign-path: a value carrying one legitimate path beside one foreign one still fires' both PASS from the real checkout and FAIL from a clone, because what counts as 'this repo's own root' moves with the checkout. A third, 'source: every UUID in a tracked SOURCE_SCANNABLE file is on the synthetic allowlist', fails EVERYWHERE with 'the walk collected no file under proxy/' — lifecycle has no proxy/ directory. Record: opus-lc58-47 lane reported 3 failures from its clone, desk reproduced the split, 2026-09-15.
goal: every-refusal-red-first
write-set: test/absence-scan.test.mjs
done-criterion: The battery returns the SAME verdict from a clone and from the real checkout, and any test that cannot is deleted rather than left failing. RED-FIRST IS ALREADY IN HAND AND IS THE PAIR ITSELF, reproduce it: `node --test test/absence-scan.test.mjs` from /home/g/dev/Gunther-Schulz/lifecycle yields ONE failing test; the same battery at the same commit from a fresh clone yields THREE. Green when both yield the same list. The two location-sensitive arms pin the repo root they mean — derived at run time from the test file's own location, never from a hardcoded prefix and never from the cwd. The proxy/ arm is a SEPARATE decision the fix must state rather than default: this repo has no proxy/ directory, so the walk is either narrowed to directories that exist here (and the arm keeps its meaning) or the arm is deleted as inherited from another repo — whichever is chosen, it is written down, because an arm failing forever is an arm nobody reads. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the scanner's REAL foreign-path detection is unchanged — a genuine foreign path still fires from both locations, asserted from both, since a location-independent battery that detects nothing would satisfy the criterion vacuously.
evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK 2026-09-15, both sides, same commit 642d483: from the real checkout `node --test test/absence-scan.test.mjs` fails ONE test (the proxy/ walk); from a fresh clone of the same repo at the same commit it fails THREE, the two extra being the named foreign-path arms. The lc-58/lc-47 lane reported the three-failure result from its private clone and correctly refused to attribute them to its own change, having measured them on a PRISTINE old clone. This desk initially saw one failure and would have called the lane's count wrong — law 17 ('a discrepancy between your count and a lane's is a claim about your instrument first') is what sent the desk to reproduce the lane's ARRANGEMENT rather than its number, and the arrangement is the whole finding. SECOND, SEPARATE MEASUREMENT, recorded so it is not conflated: `node tools/absence-scan.mjs --git-range ..HEAD` prints 'degraded: base ref is not resolvable here' for an EMPTY base and then scans the tree at HEAD plus unpushed commit interiors. The lane's 14 findings came from that degraded mode in a clone; the same command in the real checkout answers clean, and the pre-push hook runs a real range and reported clean on every push this session. So the 14 were an artefact of an empty --git-range, NOT a leak in pushed history — but a scanner that DEGRADES on a malformed range while still printing a verdict is worth its own look.
blocked-by: evidence false  # the proxy/ arm needs a read of whether that walk was inherited from claude-code-cache-fix, which owns the canonical tools/absence-scan.mjs this repo declares byte-identical; the fix must not diverge the copy without knowing
amend-reason: 2026-09-15 BLOCKER RE-POINTED AT THE CANONICAL HOME. The judgment desk routed this fix to claude-code-cache-fix, which owns tools/absence-scan.mjs and whose copy here is declared byte-identical — booked there as cf-342 with a done-criterion demanding identical battery results from checkout and clone, both runs pasted, and a notify-lifecycle clause so this copy re-syncs. That answers the read this entry was blocked on: narrowing the walk HERE would diverge a file whose whole value is being identical. THE PREDICATE IS DELIBERATELY NOT EXECUTABLE, and the first attempt at making it so is why. I wrote a grep against an ABSOLUTE path to the sibling repo's done carrier and proved it on a pair (exit 1 for cf-342 live, exit 0 for an id already closed there). It discriminated — and it broke LAW 6, no hardcoded machine path, login, repo root or XDG root ANYWHERE, in a carrier that is public history. The pre-push leak scan refused the push naming the line. I had graded the predicate as an INSTRUMENT and never asked what writing it down costs. An $HOME-relative spelling would have passed the scanner while encoding the same machine-local root, so it fails law 6 just as surely and only hides better. This carrier already holds the compliant shape for this same upstream repo: lc-53 waits on it with a false predicate and its reason, machine's court, re-evaluated each pass. The cost is a re-check by whoever next drains this repo instead of a mechanical resolution, and that is the honest price of not naming a foreign machine path in public bytes.
amended-blocked-by: 2026-09-15 evidence false  # cf-342 in claude-code-cache-fix, which owns the canonical tools/absence-scan.mjs this repo declares its copy byte-identical to, has not closed; re-checked each pass at that repo carrier
amend-reason: 2026-09-19 Re-typed from `evidence false` at the contract's build (D-8): the predicate was the literal `false`, which can never fire, so this read on the board as ordinary machine-court waiting and `item check` counted it among the unclearable softlock chains. It was never a predicate wait — nothing here can test it — and `external` is the member minted for exactly this state. It now ends by an ACT: amend the blocker away with the arrival named.

## lc-154
grade: PARKED
requirement: LAW 24's `must move with` HALF HAS NO OBSERVER AT THE MOMENT IT FIRES. The law says a write boundary is complete only once every change resolves to what REALIZES it and to every file that must MOVE WITH it, and the second half is checked today by a human noticing. For ONE measurable shape it is computable: where the change edits a SOURCE-DERIVABLE ENUMERATION, the files that derive that enumeration and grade themselves against it are greppable BEFORE a lane is briefed. Measured instance: lc-120 added a member to cli.py's `item_action` tuple; test/test_verbs.py derives that tuple from source and compares it to a hand-written DESTINATIONS table, so it went red although it realizes nothing. The cost of having no observer was one lane halt, one round trip to the desk, and one grant — with the work already built and green.
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: tools/write-set-dependents.py,test/test_tools.py
done-criterion: Given the location of a source-derivable enumeration, a tool lists the tracked files that DERIVE it and assert against it, for inclusion in a write set at booking time. PROVEN ON A PAIR drawn from the repo, never on a planted row: the KNOWN POSITIVE is cli.py's `item_action` tuple, whose answer MUST contain test/test_verbs.py — the file whose absence cost lc-120 its halt; the KNOWN NEGATIVE is an enumeration no file grades, whose answer must be empty. A tool that returns test_verbs.py for every input has not discriminated. ITS OUTPUT SET HAS THREE ANSWERS, not two: an enumeration it cannot resolve answers COULD NOT VERIFY and names what it could not read, never an empty list, because an empty list here reads as `nothing depends on this` and is the exact false clean the item exists to prevent.
evidence: Ruled a repo booking by the judgment desk on the wave-3 law-24 round, after lc-120's lane surfaced the shape and explicitly declined to generalise it: one instance, one measurement, a hypothesis rather than a class. That grade is carried here deliberately — the tool is scoped to the source-derivable-enumeration shape and claims nothing beyond it.

not-derivable: 2026-09-25 Not derivable: the freeze (LEDGER decision 2026-09-24 freeze-and-refocus) holds new mechanisms until the lc-161 after-measurement reports, and the admission bar (LEDGER 2026-09-24) needs a pre-registered probe per candidate; no ledger line answers the release yet (searched LEDGER decisions for lc-161 after and freeze release); one answer re-releases every item parked on this exact question.

## lc-155
grade: PARKED
requirement: Carrier citations rot silently in THREE kinds, each needing its own cure — measured 2026-09-17 in CachyOS-Setup ITEMS.md by the freeze-arc wave close (peer desk cachyos-setup-02, all instances verified at that repo's base 43305a3): (1) WRONG POINTER — a cited line or named artifact does not hold the claim (4 instances: a :162 for content at 435; a :42 and a :101 for content at 366-370; a named prototype script existing nowhere on disk); (2) WRONG FIGURE — a stated count diverging from the primary record ("~180 freezes" where the session's own log says 558); (3) CORRECT POINTER, STALE CONTENT — the citation resolves but the text it names has gone stale (a known-issues line naming 610.43.03 as current on a 615.71.09 box; misled a human reader for a month). Rate with its denominator: 3 of 7 explicit line/count citations wrong across one lane's five entries, concentrated in the file that changes most. A resolver-only checker catches kind 1 alone; kind 3 is invisible to it; kind 2 needs the figure re-derived from the primary record.

## lc-159
grade: STANDBY
requirement: EVERY CARRIER WRITE IS NON-ATOMIC AND A TRUNCATED CARRIER PARSES CLEAN — the tool's own worst instance of the class lc-157 is about. 16 carrier write sites (items_path/done_path/ledger_path) use Path.write_text, which truncates then writes; no atomic helper exists in the package (the sole os.replace is a docstring at declaration.py:1290). An interrupt between truncate and flush leaves the carrier SHORT, and the parse contract keys on the head, so the short file returns refused=False with FEWER items and NO problems. Every downstream consumer then gets a confident, clean, WRONG answer over a smaller carrier — the tool's deliberate third answer (unknown/refuse) is routed around entirely.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/retire.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/ledger.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,test/test_verbs.py
done-criterion: one atomic-write helper (sibling tmp in the same directory then os.replace, which is atomic on a same-filesystem rename) is the ONLY carrier write path, and all 16 sites use it; a reader sees the old carrier or the new one, never a half. RED-FIRST: a write interrupted between truncate and flush leaves the carrier intact at its previous content, proven by a fixture that kills mid-write; and the current code must FAIL that same arm. SECOND HALF, separately gradeable: truncation is made LOUD rather than quiet — a terminator or an entry count the parse checks against what it actually found — so a short carrier returns the third answer instead of a smaller clean one.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk 2026-09-18 on this repo's own ITEMS.md (223483 bytes, 71 items): truncated to 400 bytes the parse returns refused=False, 1 item, 0 problems; to 2000 bytes 2 items 0 problems; to 20000 bytes 8 items 0 problems. Zero problems at every block-boundary cut, so truncation is invisible to the parser. Positive control: the full carrier parses to 71 items with lc-158 present — an earlier probe returning 0 items for the FULL file was a dead instrument, corrected before any zero was believed. Reported from outside this repo by peer session cachyos-setup-33, reads only, who measured the downstream consequence on CachyOS-Setup's capture-retention guard: full carrier -> ('cited','cs-42'); first 2000 bytes -> ('not-cited', None), exit 0, DELETE — a guard whose purpose is protecting irreplaceable captures reporting a clean run while destroying one. THAT consequence is peer testimony, not re-run here; the parser measurements above are this desk's own.
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 2026-09-18 the blocking premise is dead, measured not argued. Half 2 was scoped as a carrier-format change (a terminator or a checked count), which under law 25 would have shipped a schema migration dry-run over every declared repo, touching carriers in repos this desk does not own and that have live sessions in them. That is why it was held. It is unnecessary: the count the format change would have ADDED is already in the head and already survives truncation, and the conservation function already fires on it. The remaining work is internal wiring on the read path, no schema bump, no migration, no other repo touched
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-18 HALF 1 DONE (65ef1cd): one atomic write path, all 18 carrier writes through it, red-first proved by two scoped mutations plus a source-derived absence guard with a planted positive control. HALF 2 RESCOPED 2026-09-18 and it is NOT a format change: make truncation LOUD at the READ. The detector already exists and already discriminates. Measured at this desk: the carrier HEAD survives truncation intact (schema/baseline/added/compacted sit at the top of the file), so the stored identity items + done == baseline + added - compacted breaks the moment a carrier is cut, and items.conservation() returns ok=False at 400 bytes (1 item) and at 20000 bytes (8 items) and ok=True on the intact 74-item carrier. What is missing is only WHERE it runs: conservation is evaluated by item check, not on the read path every other consumer uses, so a truncated carrier still parses refused=False with fewer items for every verb and every external reader. Done when a carrier read that fails conservation cannot return a quiet smaller answer: the third answer (COULD NOT VERIFY / refuse) is what consumers get, red-first proved by a truncated fixture that today returns a clean short parse and must afterwards refuse.

## lc-161
grade: READY
requirement: MEASURE DECISION ABSORPTION, NOT ONLY ERROR CATCHING. Operator, 2026-09-18, their own account of why they run desk/peer heavily: it lets them LEAVE. The peer poses decisions, the desk answers them, and the work continues without the operator, who has learned from experience roughly how far the desk's judgment can be trusted. That makes the primary measurable value DECISION ABSORPTION rather than error catching, and the catch study now running (lc-157 arc) measures a secondary effect. It also explains why no comparison has ever been available: the counterfactual to a desk/peer pair is not one session doing the same work, it is ONE SESSION THAT STALLS waiting for the operator. The corpus already carries the principle this operationalizes, the kind split, where work divides between desk and operator by kind and operator attention neither scales nor parallelizes; desk/peer is that split made structural.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: UNKNOWN. A second extraction pass over the same 9-session sample the catch study used; the instrument is the same extractor, the classification is different
done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending design, but the shape is known: per desk/peer session pair, count decisions the PEER POSED and classify each as ABSORBED (the desk answered it and work continued), ESCALATED (it reached the operator anyway), or STALLED (work waited). The ratio of absorbed to escalated is the decision-absorption rate, and unlike the catch study this needs no control arm to be meaningful, because a stall is observable in the transcript as elapsed time with no progress.
evidence: Operator, 2026-09-18, first-hand and in their own framing: they use desk/peer 'so i can leave the desk to make decisions that the peer poses so it runs on its own because i know roughly how far i can trust its judgement (from experience)'. Same sample is already characterized: 168 sessions carry peer traffic, 3203 peer messages and 6799 operator interjections machine-wide; the 9-session study sample each have at least 20 peer and 20 operator messages. TRUST CALIBRATION IS THE UNMEASURED HALF and is named here rather than folded in: the operator's confidence is built from experience across many sessions, is not transferable to a new desk, and nothing in the corpus or the tool records what it is calibrated ON. That is a separate question and probably a separate item.
blocked-by: decision should the absorption pass run over the same sample as the catch study or a fresh one?
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 2026-09-18 operator raised long-running single sessions as a counterexample (~/wan2gp), and it measured out: autonomy without a desk, apparently bought by persisted guidance instead. Folded in here rather than minted separately because it answers THIS item's question — what makes work continue without the operator — with a second mechanism, and splitting it would put two answers to one question in two carriers
amended-evidence: 2026-09-18 Operator, 2026-09-18, first-hand: they use desk/peer 'so i can leave the desk to make decisions that the peer poses so it runs on its own because i know roughly how far i can trust its judgement (from experience)'. SECOND ROUTE TO THE SAME OUTCOME, operator-raised and MEASURED at this desk the same day, and it is a counterexample to any reading that makes the pairing necessary: ~/wan2gp runs long autonomous SINGLE sessions. Operator interjections per 100 assistant turns, from the raw transcripts: 3339 turns / 135 interjections = 4.0; 1680 / 81 = 4.8; 684 / 6 = 0.9; 323 / 0 = 0.0 — with peer traffic at 0 to 43 messages, i.e. little or none. So hundreds to thousands of turns run with almost no operator input and no desk. The operator's own hypothesis for why, which the persisted artifacts support: that repo carries unusually rich persisted guidance — CLAUDE.md 553 lines, BACKLOG.md 608KB (2.7x lifecycle's entire item carrier), plus BEGEHUNG-MAP.md. READING: persisted guidance and a desk are two routes to the same property, autonomy, because both let the session answer a question WITHOUT the operator — one by querying a record, the other by asking a party. That is the research note's 'persistence guides by mere existence' claim, which the literature scout found NOBODY measures (gap 1 of 3, the named missing ablation being the arm where a record is present but never referenced). This is the first operational evidence for it on this machine and it arrived as a counterexample rather than as a confirmation, which is why it is worth more than a supporting datum. UNMEASURED and not to be assumed: whether wan2gp's sessions are as RELIABLE as they are autonomous — turn count and interjection rate say nothing about whether the output was right, and the catch study's instrument cannot see a single session's self-catches at all. TRUST CALIBRATION remains the unmeasured half of the desk route: the operator's confidence is built from experience across many sessions, is not transferable to a new desk, and nothing records what it is calibrated on.
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 DECIDED AT THIS DESK: the SAME 9-session sample as the catch study. It is already characterized (inbound counts per session, governance status, domain, operator-message volume), the extractor exists and is positive-controlled, and a fresh sample would buy a new characterization for no stated gain. The known limitation rides with it and is already recorded: governed and ungoverned repos in that sample share no domain coordinate, so the absorption figures will be indicative per session rather than a population parameter, and must be reported that way.

The three candidate sources differ in cost: head counters are a SCHEMA change, so law 25 makes them a migration dry-run over every declared repo; cumulative live(g)+done(g) is a flow only once the 8 baseline entries predating the counter are declared in or out; the fire log carries admission events already. cmd_item_ratio's own docstring rules out the easy substitute: 'a count of live items is a STOCK and would answer a different question'

## lc-165
grade: STANDBY
requirement: AN AMENDED ENTRY HEAD KEEPS THE SUPERSEDED VALUE AND A READER WHO STOPS EARLY GETS THE WRONG STORY. Amendments stack below the original, so the body a reader REACHES FIRST is the one no longer true. Measured in operation today by a peer desk: cs-35 took four amendments in about 90 minutes (requirement wrong, then evidence, then the blocker twice) and now carries superseded lines above the true state; cs-48 carries two amend-reason and two amended-evidence pairs while its LIVE requirement line still describes a design a later amendment says is not buildable. This is the label-over-body class inside one entry, and the dispatch discipline already names it from the outside: a re-grade written as an adjacent record while the original head keeps its live grade, measured twice, each time costing a lane.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: the carrier RENDERS the current effective value distinctly from its history, so the body a reader reaches first is the one that is TRUE. The item slots verb already resolves amendments and is the precedent; the gap is the RAW block, which is what a human and every non-tool reader actually opens. RED-FIRST: cs-48 shape (a live requirement contradicted by its own later amendment) is the firing input, and a reader of the rendered entry must reach the amended value before the superseded one. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the amendment HISTORY stays readable, because law 8 says both reads exist since neither is sufficient, and a rendering that hides what was superseded trades one wrong story for another.
evidence: Peer desk cachyos-setup-33, 2026-09-18, from eight amendments in operation: cs-35 four amendments in about 90 minutes; cs-48 with its live requirement contradicted by its own later amendment. THIS DESK OWN INSTANCES THE SAME DAY: lc-158, lc-159 and lc-161 were each amended after booking, and lc-159 amendment reversed the scope fivefold, its original done-criterion reading as a cross-repo schema migration that the amendment kills. Law 8 two-reads rule is both the precedent and the boundary.

## lc-173
grade: STANDBY
requirement: refusals.py IS A STRUCTURAL SERIALIZER AND IT IS LAW 2 THAT MAKES IT ONE. Measured 2026-09-18 over the eight items then open: refusals.py appears in SIX of the eight write-sets, items.py in four, verbs.py and declaration.py in three each. The cause is not booking grain — it is law 2 itself, "every refusal is a registry row", so EVERY item that emits a finding must edit the one file. The consequence is that no amount of parallel dispatch buys elapsed time on finding-emitting work: worktree lanes would each add rows to refusals.py and collide at integration. The law that makes checks provable is the same law that makes the work serial, and that tradeoff is currently invisible — the join reports the collision without naming its cause, so each desk re-derives it.
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/roster.py
done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending the decision below. The shape of a fix, if one is wanted: rows live in per-area modules that the roster imports, so two items adding rows to different areas do not touch one file. The shape of NOT fixing it: the join names the cause when refusals.py is the shared member, so a desk reads "this is law 2 serializing you" rather than re-deriving it. MUST-NOT-MOVE either way: the registry stays ONE SOURCE for its two consumers (the acceptance test and --test), which is law 3, and a split that produced two rosters would trade a scheduling cost for a correctness one.
evidence: Measured at this desk 2026-09-18 while assessing whether the open set could be parallelized: refusals.py in 6 of 8 write-sets (lc-164, 167, 168, 169, 170, 172), items.py in 4, verbs.py and declaration.py in 3. Peer desk throughput on the same day, from commit timestamps: four items in about 85 minutes, roughly 21 minutes each, all serial. The peer independently routed the batch inline and serially and named the join as its basis, so two desks reached the same conclusion from the same artifact. Law 2 text: every refusal is a registry row with the input that fires it, proven red first.
blocked-by: decision is the serializer worth removing, or worth naming in the join output?
not-derivable: 2026-09-24 searched LEDGER.md, ITEMS.md, docs/2026-09-19-round-decisions.md, and plugin/ for “serializer”, “join output”, and “join”; no decision line answers whether to remove or name the serializer. (drafted by codex luna, graded at desk)
bench-reason: 2026-09-25 first bench pass (lc-294, ready_outgrows_head fired 82 unscheduled vs 29 READY exits over 7 days): decision-complete, no open arc schedules it this window - head is lc-161 and lc-256 only; returns by item promote when head_draining fires or an arc cites it

## lc-178
grade: STANDBY
requirement: A DEMAND ADDED AT A WRITE DOOR CONTAMINATES EVERY ROSTER CONTROL THAT PASSES THROUGH THAT DOOR, AND NOTHING ENUMERATES THAT POPULATION. Measured three times in one day, by the same desk, each time discovered by RUNNING rather than by looking: lc-30's row retired a neighbour's proof; lc-167's mark demand broke 67 test arms, 35 of them from one shared plant; lc-169's derivability demand broke FOUR neighbours' roster controls at once (blocker_untyped, blocker_unstorable, parked_without_typed_blocker, new_without_typed_blocker) because every one of them proves its own row with a CLEAN control that books a decision blocker. A roster control is a fixture that must pass cleanly; adding a demand at the door it walks through makes it fail for a reason that has nothing to do with the row it proves. The cost is paid at run time, after the change is written, and it is paid again by every later item touching the same door — lc-165, lc-170 and lc-171 all touch verbs the controls use.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/roster.py,tools/prove-rows.py,test/test_roster.py
done-criterion: for a new refusal at door D, the rows whose CONTROL invokes D are listable BEFORE the demand is written. OUTCOME FIRST: a session adding a door demand can read the blast radius instead of discovering it from a red suite. The computable slice is exactly that listing — the roster already declares each row's firing input and control, so the population is derivable from the roster rather than restated beside it (law 17's restated-enumeration trap: a hardcoded list beside the source it mirrors cannot age loudly). RED-FIRST: with lc-169's derivability demand as the input, the listing must name those four rows and no others — the incident IS the fixture, and a listing that names all rows or none discriminates nothing. MUST-NOT-MOVE: this is a READOUT, never a refusal. A guard that blocked a door demand until its controls were updated would fire on legitimate work (law 11), and the whole point is that the contamination is legitimate and merely unseen.
evidence: MEASURED by the peer desk lifecycle-8c 2026-09-18, three instances in one day, and CARRIED AS THEIRS on the counts: lc-30 (a row addition retiring blocker_predicate_broken's proof, repaired in code not in the arrangement), lc-167 (67 arms, 35 from one shared plant), lc-169 (four named roster controls). THEIR OWN READING, adopted as this item's requirement because it is the generalisation and they reached it from the instances rather than from a rule: the roster's controls are a population nothing enumerates, and they were found by running rather than by looking. DERIVED AT THIS DESK: this is law 26's first clause pointed at a design-time question — what must be WRITTEN (here, listed) at the moment a door demand is added, whose absence is computable — and lc-30's own laws-file paragraph already describes the single-row case ('adding a row can retire a neighbour's proof, and only prove-rows says so'); what is new is that the population is not one neighbour but every control walking that door. LIVE RELEVANCE, not historical: lc-165, lc-170 and lc-171 are queued and all touch verbs the controls use, so the next three items are expected instances.
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 lc-171 is the fourth instance and the first where the red-first arm passed while reach was broken
amended-evidence: 2026-09-18 MEASURED by the peer desk lifecycle-8c 2026-09-18, now FOUR instances in one day and the fourth is the sharpest because it was that desk's OWN work rather than inherited: lc-30 (a row addition retiring blocker_predicate_broken's proof), lc-167 (67 arms, 35 from one shared plant), lc-169 (four named roster controls at one door), and lc-171 — a new declaration check that over-fired and put 22 ROSTER ROWS into COULD NOT VERIFY in a single run, because every declaration row runs kind check over a SCRATCH repo while the check read the machine's real desk-state home. ## lc-180
grade: PARKED
requirement: CONSERVATION SAYS A BODY LEFT AND CANNOT SAY WHEN OR WHICH, SO THE DIAGNOSIS IS HAND ARCHAEOLOGY EVERY TIME AND EVERY REPO'S BOOKINGS STOP UNTIL SOMEONE DOES IT. Measured in operation 2026-09-18 at the dotfiles carrier: 'the carrier identity is SHORT by 1 (items 127 + done 237 = 364, but baseline 241 + added 124 - compacted 0 = 365)'. The refusal is CORRECT and is the mechanism working — it caught a real loss and refused the commit. But it names only the ARITHMETIC, and the arithmetic is a whole-carrier property, so a desk holding it knows a body is gone and has no way to learn which one or when without walking git by hand. THE SECOND-ORDER COST IS THE REAL ONE: a desk attempting that walk re-implements the identity beside the checker, which is the mirrored-value drift law 17 warns about, and the dotfiles desk did exactly that and got a nonsense SHORT-by-108 from a counter that read the archive region as 0 bodies. They correctly refused to report it. The tool is the only sound instrument for its own identity, and it does not expose the historical query.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/cli.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_verbs.py
done-criterion: a verb walks the carrier's own git history, evaluates the conservation identity AT EACH COMMIT using the SAME code path the live check uses, and names the first commit where the identity went SHORT, with the ids present before and absent after. OUTCOME FIRST: a desk facing a SHORT carrier gets a commit and an id, not a bisect to perform. THE INSTRUMENT MUST BE THE TOOL ITSELF — the historical evaluation reuses the live parser and the live identity, never a reimplementation, because a second implementation of the arithmetic is the defect this item exists to stop anyone writing. RED-FIRST: the dotfiles carrier at its current HEAD is the firing input and must name a commit and an id; a carrier whose identity is CLEAN at every commit reports CLEAN with its examined-commit count per lc-172, never silence. MUST-NOT-MOVE: it REPORTS and never repairs — restoring a body is a judgment about whether it was closed, hand-deleted or merged away, and item repair's 'never invents' boundary stays exactly where it is. A verb that guessed the disposition would write a closure nobody made.
evidence: MEASURED in operation 2026-09-18 by the dotfiles desk, with the refusal text quoted above from their run; the same shortfall appeared in that session's START BANNER hours before they touched anything, so it predates both today's id collision and lc-177 and is not fallout from either. THEIR INSTRUMENT FAILURE IS THE SHARPEST EVIDENCE and is carried as their measurement: a hand counter beside the checker returned SHORT by 108 at a 2026-09-15 commit because it read the archive region as 0 bodies — a wrong answer shaped exactly like a finding, which they identified as such and refused to report rather than booking. DERIVED at this desk: the identity is a whole-carrier scalar, so it is diagnostic of EXISTENCE and silent about LOCATION, and nothing in the verb set closes that gap; law 26's first clause asks what must be WRITTEN at the moment the refusal fires, and the answer is the commit and the id, both computable from the carrier's own history. CONSUMER, named because it is live rather than hypothetical: every booking in the dotfiles repo is currently blocked behind this, including one held entry that re-runs in a single paste once the carrier is sound.

not-derivable: 2026-09-25 Not derivable: the freeze (LEDGER decision 2026-09-24 freeze-and-refocus) holds new mechanisms until the lc-161 after-measurement reports, and the admission bar (LEDGER 2026-09-24) needs a pre-registered probe per candidate; no ledger line answers the release yet (searched LEDGER decisions for lc-161 after and freeze release); one answer re-releases every item parked on this exact question.

amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-18 a verb walks the carrier's own git history, evaluates the conservation identity AT EACH COMMIT using the SAME code path the live check uses, and names the first commit where the identity went SHORT, with the ids present before and absent after. OUTCOME FIRST: a desk facing a SHORT carrier gets a commit and an id, not a bisect to perform. THE INSTRUMENT MUST BE THE TOOL ITSELF — the historical evaluation reuses the live parser and the live identity, never a reimplementation. RED-FIRST: the dotfiles carrier is the firing input and must name 92e6d9c and df-256; a carrier CLEAN at every commit reports CLEAN with its examined-commit count per lc-172, never silence. MUST-NOT-MOVE (1): it REPORTS and never repairs — restoring a body is a judgment about whether it was closed, hand-deleted or merged away, and item repair's 'never invents' boundary stays where it is. MUST-NOT-MOVE (2), MEASURED IN THE MANUAL WALK AND NOT PREDICTED: the per-commit probe must handle the GIT-WORK-TREE PRECONDITION or it will do to its own walk exactly what it exists to prevent. item check REFUSES outside a work tree ('not inside a git work tree, and no --repo was given. Refusing rather than guessing'), and a grep for the conservation line over that refusal returns NOTHING and reads as a clean commit. So each probe either inits a work tree, passes --repo, or reports COULD NOT VERIFY FOR THAT COMMIT — a refusal is never counted as a reading, and a walk that cannot evaluate a commit says so per commit rather than skipping it into the clean pile.

amended-evidence: 2026-09-18 MEASURED in operation 2026-09-18 by the dotfiles desk; the bisect it forced has now RUN by hand and located the defect, so this item's red-first is a real case rather than a constructed one: the body left at 92e6d9c, which removed df-256 from ITEMS.md and appended its body to ITEMS-DONE.md at EOF — BELOW the '## Archive (pre-migration)' heading, where bodies are held verbatim. One misplaced append caused BOTH the id reuse and the conservation short. Repair proven red-to-green on the real carrier at 15300b1: items 127 + done 238 = 365 against baseline 241 + added 124, CLEAN. THE INSTRUMENT LESSON IS THE ITEM'S REASON FOR EXISTING, and it is now FOUR MEASURED INSTANCES IN ONE DAY OF ONE SHAPE — a well-behaved REFUSAL read as a clean result by a caller reading only stdout: (1) item check refusing outside a git work tree during the bisect, where a grep for the conservation line returned nothing and read as a clean commit; (2) item ready refusing an id-less run, where a grep for the new id returned nothing and read as a missing booking that was in fact correctly booked; (3) the ugrep-flag class already in the corpus bindings, where a subprocess caller reading only stdout takes an instrument failure for a verified absence; (4) the archive-region miscount, where a hand counter read 0 bodies and produced a confident wrong SHORT-by-108 that its author correctly refused to report. DERIVED: the common factor is not the walk and not git — it is that ANY grep over a tool's OUTPUT owes a known positive, because a refusal and a clean zero are byte-indistinguishable downstream. A desk attempting the diagnosis by hand also re-implements the identity beside the checker, which is law 17's mirrored-value drift and is what produced instance (4).

## lc-189
grade: STANDBY
requirement: THE PRODUCER ROUTE IS UNFALSIFIABLE IN ONE DIRECTION. declaration.py builds the producer pool by scanning every kind WRITER, then resolves producer: references against that same set — so a producer named in a writer can NEVER dangle, while the identical reference in a reader correctly FINDS. An expectation derived from the artifact it grades moves with the mutant and stays green on the corruption it exists to catch. It matters more than its size because the route-set check counts producer as a WATCHED route, so both the roster green and the route-set green hold over the hole.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,test/test_declaration.py
done-criterion: a producer: reference that names nothing is a finding WHEREVER it appears, including in a writer — the pool a reference is checked against is not built from the same field the reference sits in. RED-FIRST: a declaration whose writer names a producer that exists nowhere else reads CLEAN today and must be a finding after; the reader-side spelling keeps finding it exactly as it does now. MUST-NOT-MOVE: a legitimate writer naming a real producer stays clean, and the route-set check keeps counting producer as watched, since after this it will be watched in both directions rather than one.
evidence: RELAYED from the review lane via lifecycle-6f, carried as theirs: declaration.py 1184-1198 and 1258-1271, the pool built from writers and the references resolved against it, graded real but narrow. DERIVED: this is the same-parentage rule in the corpus, an expectation read off the body it grades, and the route-set green is what hides it — the roster can be green and the coverage complete while one direction of the route can never fail.
blocked-by: NONE
bench-reason: 2026-09-25 first bench pass (lc-294, ready_outgrows_head fired 82 unscheduled vs 29 READY exits over 7 days): decision-complete, no open arc schedules it this window - head is lc-161 and lc-256 only; returns by item promote when head_draining fires or an arc cites it

amended-evidence: 2026-10-07 RELAYED from wave A lane F 2026-10-07 at base 3eb0f98, not re-run at the desk: ref_world (declaration.py near 1666) builds the producer pool only from kind writers, and no other producer registry exists in the declaration or plugin.json (grep). DERIVED: making a writer-named producer dangle needs an INDEPENDENT pool, which is a new declaration field, a schema change, outside the defect-repair exemption of the 2026-09-24 freeze. Not buildable as a repair.
amend-reason: 2026-10-07 reproduced; the registry shape is ruled
amended-evidence: 2026-10-07 RELAYED from wave E lane E8 2026-10-07 at 83eaa73: reproduced (a writer naming an unknown producer gives no finding, the same spelling in a reader gives dangling_reference). The only producer reference in ten rostered declarations is this repo .claude/lifecycle.json writer producer:plugin-installer. ## lc-194
grade: STANDBY
requirement: A SHELL ANSWERS FOR THE LAST PROCESS ONLY, AND THIS STACK ASKS THE SHELL FOR VERDICTS IN SEVERAL PLACES. Two independently built review lanes hit this from opposite ends: a pipeline Trigger predicate makes BROKEN unreachable, because find piped to grep -q returns the LAST stage status, so a broken find exits 1 and renders QUIET — a dead lane as a clean board; and verify.py reports RAN, clean for a command whose first stage does not exist, while the bare and the and-chained spellings correctly give COULD NOT VERIFY. The same evaluator serves evidence blockers, so a broken pipeline reads as still blocked forever. THE CLASS IS THE FINDING: wherever an exit code crosses a shell boundary the code belongs to the last stage and not to the thing being graded, and the members are swept for rather than the two known sites repaired.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/lanes.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verify.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_lanes.py,test/test_verify.py
done-criterion: every site where this stack takes a verdict from a shell is ENUMERATED, and each one either stops trusting the last-stage code or declares in its own output that it cannot discriminate. THE ENUMERATION IS THE DELIVERABLE, not the two known repairs: the sweep is keyed on the invariant every member carries — a subprocess whose returncode becomes a verdict — never on the idiom the two found instances happen to share. RED-FIRST, both measured by the lanes: a pipeline whose FIRST stage is broken must not read QUIET as a trigger nor RAN-clean as a verify; the bare and and-chained spellings keep their current answers exactly. MUST-NOT-MOVE: a legitimate pipeline predicate that WORKS keeps firing and staying quiet as it does today — the repair cannot be to forbid pipelines, which would fire on every legitimate lane using one.
evidence: RELAYED from two independent review lanes via lifecycle-6f, carried as theirs and as the strongest grade available here because the lanes varied the axis that matters: lane 1 found it in lanes.py Trigger evaluation by reasoning, lane 2 RAN it against verify.py with four spellings and reported which two diverge. DERIVED, and it is why this is one item rather than two: both are the same mechanism — a shell reports the last process status — so repairing the two sites and not sweeping for the rest would leave the class intact at every site neither lane happened to read.
blocked-by: NONE

## lc-198
grade: STANDBY
requirement: A SILENTLY SKIPPED ARM IS A REACH ARM DELETED, AND THE SUITE STILL EXITS 0. One arm in test_verbs is premised on a sibling dotfiles checkout and vanishes anywhere that checkout is absent — a fresh clone, another machine, a CI runner — while unittest discover still exits 0 and the repo verify section reads only the exit code. That arm is blocker_unstorable REACH case, so where it vanishes the roster still reads 110 green while one refusal is graded by nothing. A skip that removes a reach arm and leaves a green is the third answer collapsing into the first, in the suite this repo trusts most.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: test/test_verbs.py,tools/verify-suite.py,CLAUDE.md
done-criterion: a SKIPPED arm is visible in the verdict rather than folded into a pass: the suite reports skips as their own count and a run whose skips include a reach arm does not read as a clean run. THE COUNT COMES OFF THE RESULT OBJECT, never the -v rendering, per law 17 — res.skipped and t.id(), which is how the lane measured it. RED-FIRST: rename the sibling checkout and the suite must stop reading as clean where today it exits 0 with one arm gone. MUST-NOT-MOVE: an arm that legitimately cannot run somewhere is still allowed to skip — the repair is that the skip is REPORTED, never that the arm is forced to run or deleted; and the arms that do run keep their verdicts unchanged.
evidence: RELAYED from review lane 2 via lifecycle-6f, carried as theirs and read off the RESULT OBJECT rather than console output, which is the discrimination law 17 demands: the arm is premised on ../dotfiles and disappears without it. DERIVED: the repo declared verify set reads exit codes, so a vanished arm is invisible to the one instrument anyone runs before trusting the package.

## lc-201
grade: STANDBY
requirement: A GIT WORKTREE REGISTRATION IS A PERSISTED THING THAT RESOLVES TO NO REGISTERED KIND, AND IT LIVES WHERE THE SWEEP CANNOT REACH. Measured 2026-09-18 in this repo: git worktree list shows /tmp/claude-1000/-home-g-dev-Gunther-Schulz-lifecycle/55c5d05e-.../scratchpad/wt-old at 2c65b8d (detached), a frozen reader from another session. Its record lives in .git/worktrees/, which the unregistered-persisted-thing sweep never walks because that sweep reads git ls-files — tracked files only. Invariant 1 says every persisted thing resolves to a registered kind; this population sits outside the only world-facing instrument the repo has.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/retire.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_retire.py
done-criterion: The sweep's could-not-verify arm NAMES git worktree registrations as a population it does not examine, or a check reports them. MUST-NOT-BUILD: nothing REMOVES or prunes a worktree. What a party is standing in is not moved under them, a live session may hold this one, and its directory still exists so prune would not touch the entry anyway. The deliverable is that the registration stops being invisible, never that it stops existing. RED-FIRST: with a worktree registered the check names it; with none registered the check says so rather than reporting clean by silence.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk, executed: git worktree list returns two entries, the second under a foreign session's scratchpad at a detached head. RELAYED from lifecycle-6f, carried as theirs and unverified here: session 55c5d05e is the writer-reservation lock holder this repo has reported all evening and may be live; ls confirms the directory exists, so git worktree prune would not clear the entry — prune only clears entries whose path is gone. DERIVED, not executed: that the sweep cannot see .git/worktrees/ follows from its reading tracked files; the arm that would report the absence has not been run against a registered worktree.

THE DERIVATION IS THE POINT, NOT THE LIST: a restated enumeration beside the code it mirrors cannot age loudly, the source gains a fifth variable and the published set stays green, byte-identical to health. The existing instance to build against is this repo's own law 24 worked example — test_verbs.py holds a hand-written dispatch table that derives the action tuple from source and grades itself against it, which the laws file names as the anchor rule's GOOD form. That table resolves a DECLARED home; it does not enumerate what the tool CONSUMES, so this item adds the second question rather than duplicating the first. ## lc-215
grade: STANDBY
requirement: run_schema's 'in every carrier' MEANS EXACTLY THREE KINDS, AND BOTH SIDES OF ITS AGREEMENT CHECK SHARE THAT BLIND SPOT. declaration.carrier_homes covers items, done bodies and ledger lines only, with ledger lines defaulting to LEDGER.md UNCONDITIONALLY. Two consequences: (a) a declaring repo that registers no ledger-lines kind and has no LEDGER.md at root gets COULD NOT VERIFY at migrate.py:1937 and the whole schema migration refuses BEFORE printing any plan; (b) the CLEAN at 1973-1975 says the repo is 'already at schema N in the declaration and in every carrier' over those three kinds only. check_schema_agreement resolves through the SAME function, so both sides of that comparison share the blind spot — an expectation derived from the artifact it grades.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,test/test_schema.py
