The unconditional LEDGER.md default is decided: either it is justified in the code's own words or a repo without that kind stops being refused for lacking a file it never declared. MUST-NOT-BUILD: this is not a licence to widen carrier_homes silently — widening it changes what every caller resolves, and the dependents question is part of the work. RED-FIRST: a declaring repo with no ledger-lines kind and no LEDGER.md currently cannot run a schema migration at all; after, it either migrates or is told precisely why not.
evidence: RELAYED from the lc-192 lane, DERIVED on its side and NOT constructed: it read carrier_homes and run_schema and explicitly said it did not build the repo population that would exercise (a). MEASURED at this desk and it is the reason this is not hypothetical: my own lc-205 reproduction hit consequence (a) twice — a scratch repo with no ITEMS-DONE.md refused with 'COULD NOT VERIFY: the done bodies carrier could not be read for its schema line', and with no schema head on LEDGER.md refused again with the ledger-lines equivalent, both BEFORE any plan printed. I built those fixtures to reproduce a different defect and was stopped by this one, which is the strongest evidence available that the population is reachable rather than theoretical. DERIVED: this is the audit's finding-4 shape one level out — both sides of a comparison resolving through one function cannot detect that function's omission.

## lc-216
grade: STANDBY
requirement: LAW 2 SAYS EVERY REFUSAL IS A REGISTRY ROW, AND THE CODE ADMITS 3 COULD-NOT-VERIFY REFUSALS OUT OF 196 EMISSIONS. THE QUESTION IS WHETHER THE LAW'S REACH IS NARROWER THAN ITS TEXT OR THE REGISTRY HAS A POPULATION IT NEVER ADMITTED — AND IT MUST NOT BE ANSWERED BY ADDING A ROW. This repo's whole discipline says could-not-verify is a FIRST-CLASS verdict, not a soft finding. Law 2 is written over refusals without saying which verdicts count. Today's ruling already moved the line once: roster.py:65-74 records that the emit-site scan matched 'FINDING [' alone, so a refusal emitted as COULD NOT VERIFY was invisible to it, and _LITERAL_CNV was added. Three sites took the bracket. 193 did not. Nothing decides which of the 193 are refusals in law 2's sense, and because the scanner keys on the BRACKET, an unbracketed could-not-verify is outside the registry's reach BY CONSTRUCTION — it cannot be counted as missing, so the coverage check's denominator has never included it. THE ANSWER DECIDES WHETHER THE ROSTER'S DENOMINATOR HAS BEEN WRONG ALL ALONG, which is why adding one row would bury the question rather than settle it.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: CLAUDE.md,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/roster.py
done-criterion: Law 2's reach over the third answer is DECIDED and written where the law is, in one of exactly two directions, each with a consequence that is then built. EITHER the law covers findings only and says so — in which case the three bracketed could-not-verify rows are the anomaly and the scanner's CNV matcher is documented as an opt-in channel rather than a coverage claim. OR could-not-verify refusals are registry-bearing — in which case the registry has 193 unadmitted emissions and the coverage check's assurance is WIDER THAN ITS PREDICATE, which is this repo's own label-over-body class at the instrument that grades every other one. MUST-NOT-BUILD, and it is the whole point: this is NOT resolved by adding a roster row for any site, mine included. A row would make one site green and leave the denominator question exactly where it was. RED-FIRST for whichever direction wins: the coverage check states in its own printed LIMIT which verdict channels it covers, and a planted emission in an UNCOVERED channel must NOT be reported as covered — an assertion on what must not come back, since the failure here is an assurance reading wider than it is.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk, executed, with a positive control: 'COULD NOT VERIFY' appears 196 times across the core package and 'COULD NOT VERIFY [' — the bracketed, registry-bearing form — exactly 3; the control 'FINDING [' returns 125, so the pattern discriminates rather than matching nothing. The three are retire.py:143 home_unresolvable, verify.py:152 verify_check_did_not_run, verbs.py:1373 goal_query_undeclared, and ALL THREE resolve in the roster. The unbracketed 193 are spread across verbs.py 39, cli.py 33, migrate.py 31, items.py 20, retire.py 18, refusals.py 13 and six more files. MEASURED mechanism: roster.py:74 defines _LITERAL_CNV to match the bracketed form, and its own comment at 65-66 records the ruling — 'This scan matched FINDING [ alone, so a refusal emitted as COULD NOT VERIFY was invisible to it' — dated 2026-09-18, so the line moved TODAY and this item asks where it now sits. THIS ITEM CORRECTS A PREMISE I ACTED ON: I left refusals.py out of lc-205's built write-set citing lc-33's evidence that a grep for 'COULD NOT VERIFY [' returns ZERO across the core package. That measurement was taken 2026-09-13 and is now false — it returns 3. The decision may still be right; the basis I gave for it was stale, and lc-205's new could-not-verify site is itself one of the 193. RELAYED from lifecycle-6f, who framed the question and explicitly asked that it be booked as a question about the LAW rather than as a missing row.
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 Records the catch mechanism, at lifecycle-6f's request and for the reason they gave: a self-report that says WHAT CAUGHT IT tells the next reader what to build, while one that says only what was wrong asks them to be more careful. It was a second errand crossing the same fact, not vigilance — which is an argument for re-derivation at citation time and against another remember-to rule.
amended-evidence: 2026-09-18 MEASURED at this desk, executed, with a positive control: 'COULD NOT VERIFY' appears 196 times across the core package and the bracketed registry-bearing form 'COULD NOT VERIFY [' exactly 3; the control 'FINDING [' returns 125, so the pattern discriminates rather than matching nothing. The three are retire.py:143 home_unresolvable, verify.py:152 verify_check_did_not_run, verbs.py:1373 goal_query_undeclared, and all three resolve in the roster. The unbracketed 193 span verbs.py 39, cli.py 33, migrate.py 31, items.py 20, retire.py 18, refusals.py 13 and six more files. INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED by lifecycle-6f: 3 / 196 / 125, same three sites. MEASURED mechanism: roster.py:74 defines _LITERAL_CNV matching the bracketed form, and its comment at 65-66 records the ruling — 'This scan matched FINDING [ alone, so a refusal emitted as COULD NOT VERIFY was invisible to it' — dated 2026-09-18. SO THE QUESTION IS NOT PERMISSION BUT THE DENOMINATOR, and that is sharper than the question as first asked: the repo ALREADY decided a could-not-verify can be registry-bearing, and because the scanner keys on the BRACKET, 193 of 196 sites are outside the registry's reach BY CONSTRUCTION — they cannot be counted as missing, so the coverage check's denominator has never included them. A coverage check whose denominator excludes what it cannot see is an assurance wider than its predicate. THIS ITEM CORRECTS A PREMISE I ACTED ON: I left refusals.py out of lc-205's built write-set citing lc-33's ZERO, measured 2026-09-13. The decision stands; the reason was stale, and lc-205's closure now carries a forward pointer saying so rather than leaving the sentence to be read as current. HOW THE STALE PREMISE WAS CAUGHT, RECORDED BECAUSE IT DECIDES WHAT TO BUILD RATHER THAN WHAT TO REMEMBER: not by vigilance and not by any rule firing. I cited a real measurement, from a real carrier, with a real id, and every one of those facts is what made it invisible — nothing in the sentence was wrong except its DATE, so a premise and its dependent sat in one output contradicting only across time. What caught it was a SECOND ERRAND crossing the same fact: I re-ran the grep hours later for a different question and the number had moved. The corpus names this class and the naming did not catch it, which is the argument for a mechanism that re-derives a cited count at the moment it is cited rather than a rule asking anyone to re-check.

## lc-217
grade: STANDBY
requirement: A COMPLETED CARRIER MIGRATION THAT LEAVES ITS SOURCE BEHIND IS AN UNFINISHED MIGRATION, AND NOTHING SAYS SO. Operator ruling 2026-09-18, quoted first-hand via lifecycle-6f: 'wehn you migrate dont leave legacy fiels lying around thast someting thast important. avfter migration only the new satte shdlu exists. for legacy evergting is in git anyways'. The operator's own basis is what makes it safe — git holds every prior state, so retiring the source destroys nothing and the reversible test passes at the REPO rather than only at the desk. Today retiring is OPT-IN, so the default outcome of a carrier migration is two states coexisting and no mechanism anywhere calls that wrong. THE FIX IS POLARITY, NOT FORCE: this gates the SILENCE, never the ACT.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_migrate_residue.py
done-criterion: A completed carrier migration whose SOURCE FILE STILL EXISTS reports a FINDING naming the source path and the exit it has not taken. MUST-NOT-BUILD, and it is the heart of the ruling: this is NOT delete-by-default. The four existing preconditions on --retire-source stay EXACTLY as they are and none is softened — retire_source_uncommitted, retire_source_laws_absent, retire_source_unpinned_anchor, retire_source_not_writing — because they gate the irreversible ACT and deleting past them would be a destructive step sized to intent rather than to the object's current state. What changes is that the tool stops being silent about a migration that stopped half way: an operator who genuinely wants both states keeps them and now has to say so. RED-FIRST: a completed migration leaving BACKLOG.md in place currently exits CLEAN and must report the finding; a migration whose source was retired stays CLEAN; and the CONTROL that bounds it — a run BLOCKED by any of the four preconditions must report THAT precondition, not this new finding, since telling an operator their migration is unfinished when the tool itself refused to finish it would be a guard firing on legitimate work (law 11). THE SWEEP IS PART OF THE ITEM: already-migrated declared repos are checked for a surviving source, and each is disposed or explicitly recorded as deliberate.
evidence: RELAYED from lifecycle-6f, operator words quoted verbatim above and carried as the operator's, not paraphrased. MEASURED at this desk: the four preconditions are real and named — retire_source_uncommitted, retire_source_laws_absent, retire_source_unpinned_anchor and retire_source_not_writing all appear in the roster's own listing of arrangements, and all four sit in the set prove-rows reports as carrying NO recorded mutation, which is worth knowing before anything near them is changed. MEASURED: this repo is its own first case and has ALREADY taken the exit correctly — BACKLOG.md was deleted on migration with its legacy citations pinned to the deleting commit's parent (5c257ec), which CLAUDE.md records with a measured verification that all 16 citations resolve at exactly the cited start line. So the ruling makes general a pattern this repo already demonstrates rather than inventing one. DERIVED: the carrier-migration path is where a source genuinely survives, because the schema path reads no source at all — measured separately under lc-168.
blocked-by: NONE
bench-reason: 2026-09-25 first bench pass (lc-294, ready_outgrows_head fired 82 unscheduled vs 29 READY exits over 7 days): decision-complete, no open arc schedules it this window - head is lc-161 and lc-256 only; returns by item promote when head_draining fires or an arc cites it
amend-reason: 2026-10-07 not buildable until the keep-declaration is designed
amended-evidence: 2026-10-07 RELAYED from wave E lane E7 2026-10-07 at 83eaa73: built literally the finding fires on every default writing run, because the default FREEZES the source (DISPOSED_FROZEN); a probe took the suite to 115 failures. Three decisions precede a build: how an operator declares keeping both states (flag, declaration key), whether a frozen source counts as the exit taken, and the sweep of repos already migrated. refusals.py belongs in the write-set.

## lc-220
grade: STANDBY
requirement: THE RETIREMENT TRIPWIRE COMPUTES A LIFETIME RATIO WHERE THE RULE SPECIFIES A STRETCH, SO THE ALARM GETS QUIETER THE LONGER A REPO RUNS. The corpus rule says, in its own words, a rough tripwire of 3:1 over a plus-30-percent-LINE STRETCH. A stretch, not a lifetime. item ratio states its own window in its own output — capture added since the carrier was CREATED — and offers no window flag at all. NOT AN ARITHMETIC BUG: the sum is right. It is the ANCHOR. A denominator that only accumulates means every day of healthy draining makes the next capture-heavy day harder to detect, and a repo with enough history can eventually never trip it. That is a check going stale toward PASSING — the silent direction, where nobody looks because nothing goes red — and it is the same class as the historical-finding-count test repaired earlier today, one level up.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: The ratio is computed over a WINDOW that can go red on a capture-heavy stretch in a long-lived repo, and the verb states which window it used in its own output as it does today. THE WINDOW ITSELF IS THE DECISION AND IS NOT SETTLED HERE — do not invent a rolling window to make the number move. FIRST STEP, and it may be the real subject: since-the-last-retirement-pass requires a pass to have been RECORDED, and MEASURED HERE nothing records one, so that window is keyed to an event nothing writes — the observer gap, and choosing it without building the recording act would ship a window that never advances. RED-FIRST: a carrier whose lifetime ratio is below the tripwire while its recent stretch is above it must go RED — which is exactly this repo at this commit, so the arrangement is the repo itself and needs no fixture. CONTROL that must stay green: a carrier draining steadily over the same window stays CLEAN, since the trigger reads flow and never size.
evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK, and the pair is the whole finding, same carrier same moment: item ratio reports 211:105 = 2.01:1 CLEAN over its lifetime window, while TODAY over the same carrier is 63 captured against 18 drained = 3.50:1, ABOVE the 3:1 tripwire. Both numbers are true; the rule window would fire and the tool window does not. Reproducible from git: baseline is the parent of the first ITEMS.md commit today, capture is added id headings, drain is id headings added to ITEMS-DONE.md. MY OWN FIRST COUNT WAS WRONG AND THE METHOD MATTERS, so it is recorded: a cumulative diff over the window returned 47, because an item added AND closed inside the window nets out of ITEMS.md entirely. Summing per commit returns 63. A net diff UNDER-COUNTS capture, which is the conservative direction and still wrong. RELAYED from lifecycle-6f, who found this and whose 63:18 I reproduced independently rather than carrying. MEASURED: item ratio --help offers no window flag, and grep over LEDGER.md returns ZERO mentions of a retirement pass while verbs.py only WARNS that one is owed — so nothing anywhere records a pass having happened.
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 Carrying the under-count finding into the requirement at the judgment desk request, rather than leaving it in the evidence slot as a note about my method. It is not only how I mis-measured: it is a SECOND mechanism pushing the same instrument quiet, independent of the lifetime window, and two under-counts pointing the same way in one alarm is a property of the instrument rather than an anecdote about the measurer.
amended-requirement: 2026-09-18 THE RETIREMENT TRIPWIRE COMPUTES A LIFETIME RATIO WHERE THE RULE SPECIFIES A STRETCH, SO THE ALARM GETS QUIETER THE LONGER A REPO RUNS. The corpus rule says, in its own words, a rough tripwire of 3:1 over a plus-30-percent-LINE STRETCH. A stretch, not a lifetime. item ratio states its own window in its own output — capture added since the carrier was CREATED — and offers no window flag at all. NOT AN ARITHMETIC BUG: the sum is right. It is the ANCHOR. A denominator that only accumulates means every day of healthy draining makes the next capture-heavy day harder to detect, and a repo with enough history can eventually never trip it. That is a check going stale toward PASSING — the silent direction, where nobody looks because nothing goes red. TWO INDEPENDENT UNDER-COUNTING MECHANISMS POINT THE SAME WAY IN ONE INSTRUMENT, and that is the finding beyond the arithmetic: the lifetime window quiets the alarm as history accumulates, AND the obvious way to measure a window — a cumulative diff across it — NETS OUT every item added and closed inside that window, because such an item leaves the carrier entirely and never appears as an addition. Measured: 47 by cumulative diff against 63 summed per commit, same window. Both errors are CONSERVATIVE, both make capture look smaller than it was, and an alarm cannot be made safe by erring quiet — quiet is the direction that fails silently. Same class as the historical-finding-count test repaired earlier today, one level up.
bench-reason: 2026-09-25 first bench pass (lc-294, ready_outgrows_head fired 82 unscheduled vs 29 READY exits over 7 days): decision-complete, no open arc schedules it this window - head is lc-161 and lc-256 only; returns by item promote when head_draining fires or an arc cites it
amend-reason: 2026-10-07 2026-10-07 dispatched in wave D and returned as a gap: needs the window decided before the tripwire can read it
amended-evidence: 2026-10-07 RELAYED from wave D lane D5 2026-10-07 at ec9d941, measured there: after lc-291 item ratio prints the lifetime ratio plus a seven-day net-growth window labelled PLACEHOLDER, and the defect still reproduces through refusals._ratio_history with cuts (before,100,60),(first-half,190,60),(now,190,80): lifetime 190:80 = 2.38:1, under the tripwire, while the window holds 90 captured against 20 drained, and the verdict is CLEAN because growth is not sustained across both halves. DERIVED: closing it means grading 3:1 over that placeholder window, which changes a verdict lc-291 tests (a burst that drains is not a finding) on a window nobody has measured.

DRAIN: each recurring class gets a recorded disposition, a required slot at its firing moment, or a deterministic artifact-keyed check, or HELD with a named re-open event. A class with no disposition is the carrier alarm. Operator intent first-hand 2026-09-19: one of the pillars of lifecycle I want to solve.

RESIDUE OUTSIDE THIS WRITE BOUNDARY, named not assumed: the going-forward capture seam is the machine-wide course-corrections carrier under the operator corpus, which this repo write boundary excludes, so that half is the driving desk to place and is not discharged by this entry. ADDED 2026-09-19, two halves. (a) INTERVENTION-TAXONOMY HYPOTHESES: the mining half registers its candidate classes BEFORE the pass runs, so the taxonomy is graded against a prediction instead of read off whatever the sample happens to contain, since a taxonomy fitted after the fact explains every sample and predicts none. The saturation claim is what makes this testable: if the classes are finite at about 18, a pre-registered set should capture most of a fresh sample and the fraction it MISSES is the honest measure of the taxonomy's reach. (b) DERIVABLE-FRACTION METRIC: each mined operator question is graded on whether its answer was ALREADY DERIVABLE from what the repo held at the moment it was asked. That split decides what to build, because it separates the two weak links the design of record names: an answer NOT in the record is a CAPTURE gap, one that WAS there is a RETRIEVAL gap, and one pipeline would otherwise report them as a single number.

State it as a mechanism DEFINED BY WHAT IT INDEPENDENTLY READS, never by authority over the peer, because role separation over the same information is provably dominated and only an independent SIGNAL does the work. ## lc-235
grade: PARKED
requirement: THE READY SET HOLDS ITEMS OLDER THAN WHAT LIFECYCLE IS BECOMING, AND NOTHING ASKS WHETHER THEY STILL POINT THE SAME WAY. Operator, first-hand 2026-09-19: do any of the older entries compete with or diverge from the current design direction? MEASURED at the driving desk: 114 READY split 60 at lc-150+ (this arc's own), 24 at lc-100..149 (the schema wave), and 30 below lc-100 (2026-08-26 through 09-13); the sweep population is the ~54 items below lc-150. WHY NO EXISTING MECHANISM COVERS THIS, which is why the entry exists rather than being someone's habit: pickup re-grounding checks an item's own CITED PREMISES and fires only AT pickup, so an item whose cited facts still resolve while the design of record has superseded its whole APPROACH sails through it, and an item never picked stays plausible forever. The retirement trigger reads FLOW, which is CLEAN at 2.10:1. Neither asks the DIRECTION question.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: ITEMS.md
done-criterion: Every pre-lc-150 READY item is graded against the current design of record (docs/the-loop.md, the seven-stage schema, the three-answer contract, and answerable-not-felt.md AS CORRECTED by its research companion) with exactly THREE outcomes per item: HOLDS; DIVERGES, amended or dropped with the reason recorded; or SUPERSEDED, dropped citing its successor. THE OUTPUT IS A PER-ITEM DISPOSITION LIST, NEVER A COUNT — a count of a sweep is the label-over-body class at the one artifact whose whole value is that each member was actually looked at, and a sweep reporting '54 reviewed' is indistinguishable from one that read none. MUST-NOT-MOVE: a drop here is a recorded exit of equal standing to a closure and carries its one-line reason; nothing leaves silently.
evidence: RELAYED from the driving desk cachyos-setup-b3, 2026-09-19, and carried as theirs rather than reproduced here: the 114 READY split of 60 / 24 / 30 across the three id bands, measured at that desk. The operator's question is first-hand at that desk and reaches this entry through it. MEASURED HERE, independently, at a533253: `item ratio` reads 225:107 = 2.10:1 CLEAN, which is the basis for the claim that the retirement trigger does not and will not fire on this — it reads flow, and flow is healthy, so a direction problem is invisible to it by construction. DERIVED: the pickup-re-grounding gap is read off the design of record's own mechanism 5, whose subject is an item's cited EVIDENCE rather than its approach.

## lc-236
grade: STANDBY
requirement: WAVE CONSTRUCTION CUTS LANES AND NEVER CONSOLIDATES, so a wave is built out of candidates nobody re-grounded and duplicates nobody merged. Operator direction, first-hand 2026-09-19: when items drain, wave construction is supposed to make draining EFFICIENT, so a consolidation review-and-update belongs in that phase. Ruled at the driving desk: ALWAYS, and SCOPED TO THE WAVE'S CANDIDATES, never the whole carrier — the sizing boundary is what makes it affordable, since a full-carrier pass per wave is exactly the judgment pass that grows with the queue, while a wave-scoped one is bounded by wave size. Whole-carrier work stays with the triggered passes: the retirement ratio and the divergence sweep.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: MECHANICAL SLICE, realizing in the waves join. (a) Every candidate's write-set paths are RE-RESOLVED against the live tree at join time, and a phantom or moved path is a FINDING, never a silent non-collision — a path that resolves to nothing collides with nothing by construction, so the join's worst misread currently presents as its cleanest answer. (b) Collision clusters PRINT AS MERGE QUESTIONS, so the join's collision output doubles as the consolidation worklist. RED-FIRST for (a): a candidate whose write-set names a path that does not exist must FIRE, and one whose paths all resolve must NOT — the second arm decides shippability, since a check that fires on everything is not a check. JUDGMENT SLICE, prose plus a required token: a per-candidate supersession check against recent closures and design decisions, rendered as a required line in the wave's route report — 'consolidation: N re-grounded, X merged, Y superseded' or 'nothing owed' — presence computable, fill quality judgment, per the required-slots pattern. MUST-NOT-BUILD: nothing that grades the fill's CONTENT, and no full-carrier pass — the scope boundary is the affordability argument and widening it silently is how this becomes the pass it was designed not to be. DESIGN QUESTIONS THIS ENTRY CARRIES RATHER THAN ANSWERS: the token's home (the waves verb's own output, or the route-line convention), and this item's relation to lc-173 and the test/-directory collapse.
evidence: ALL THREE BASES ARE MEASURED IN THIS REPO. (1) The join consumes write-set slots UNCHECKED: the backlog doctrine's own measured case is four existing files booked under a wrong prefix graded as sharing no file with any schedulable item, and a phantom path renders as a tidy standalone lane. (2) MEASURED AT THIS DESK 2026-09-19, and it is the sharper half: `item waves` over the current schedulable set returns ONE LANE OVER 102 ITEMS, because `test/` is a DIRECTORY entry written by 13 items and covering files named by 78 more, so the partition collapses transitively and the lane count stops discriminating. A desk reading only the lane count reads that as a correct serialization verdict. (3) Pickup re-grounding fires AFTER the wave is cut, so staleness found there costs a lane halt rather than a join-time finding. (4) RELAYED from the driving desk and carried as theirs: 55% of completed items had scope amended between booking and close, by slot evidence 39% and write-set 17% — the write-set figure is the decay the join eats directly.
blocked-by: decision has the design round closed, settling the join vocabulary and where a route-report required token lives?
amend-reason: 2026-09-19 round decision D-4: the design round answered this entry's decision blocker (token home = the waves verb's own output, ledger 2026-09-19); what remains blocking is lc-173, since a collapsed join makes any consolidation token confidently wrong - the (f) could-not-discriminate arm folds into lc-173's design
amended-blocked-by: 2026-09-19 lc-173
bench-reason: 2026-09-25 first bench pass (lc-294, ready_outgrows_head fired 82 unscheduled vs 29 READY exits over 7 days): decision-complete, no open arc schedules it this window - head is lc-161 and lc-256 only; returns by item promote when head_draining fires or an arc cites it

## lc-237
grade: PARKED
requirement: A KIND'S TRIGGER PREDICATE IS DOCUMENTED AS EVALUATED AND IS EVALUATED BY NOTHING, AND THE ONE KIND USING IT IS FIRING UNREAD. declaration.py:119 states that a kind's trigger predicate is a condition evaluated by lanes.evaluate_trigger, the ONE evaluator whose exit mapping is total so a predicate that cannot run is BROKEN and never quiet. No code does this. The seventh stage validates its vocabulary, demands a reason after none, and checks verb <name> against the parser; a predicate value is never run. So a declared predicate that has NEVER RUN and one that runs quiet are indistinguishable in the declaration, which is the same two-states-under-one-word shape the design round carries as findings (a) through (h). Routed from wave B lc-224 pickup, where the entry's plan to mirror this evaluator revealed there is no evaluation path to mirror.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: UNKNOWN
done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending the round. The shape is a choice between two named repairs and the entry must not pre-empt it: EITHER the predicate mode gains a declared-exemption form that the predicate itself reads, so the alarm means a law went uncited WITHOUT a declared reason, OR the predicate mode is retired from TRIGGER_MODES until something evaluates it. MUST-NOT-MOVE, and it is the whole point of law 11 here: the predicate is NOT softened and the firing is NOT suppressed. A check firing on declared legitimate work trains the override reflex that kills the guard, and a check nothing evaluates is not a check at all.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk 2026-09-19 by executing, not by reading. lanes.evaluate_trigger has exactly three call sites: lanes.py:680 for lane triggers, verbs.py:902 and verbs.py:1730 for item evidence blockers. None is in declaration.py. POSITIVE CONTROL for that absence: the same grep returns those three real sites, so the zero for kind triggers is a reading and not a dead pattern. THE ONE KIND USING THE MODE, from the declaration: journal entries, whose trigger is predicate grep -q 'NO JOURNAL ENTRY SUPPORTS THIS' CLAUDE.md. RUN THROUGH THE REAL EVALUATOR by this desk: state FIRE, exit 0. CONFIRMED AT THE ARTIFACT with a negative control: the string is at CLAUDE.md:108, and a deliberately near-miss variant returns 0 hits, so the instrument discriminates. WHY THIS IS LAW 11 SHAPED RATHER THAN A STALE PREDICATE: the fire may be the INTENDED semantics, since the predicate reads as a law stands uncited so a journal entry is OWED. What makes it a false alarm is that the laws file DECLARES the uncited state legitimate for laws 13, 15 and 16, each carrying its own reason for standing uncited. So if this predicate were ever wired to its documented evaluator it would be a permanent alarm on declared legitimate work, and declaration.py's own commentary warns against exactly that, calling a false-firing predicate a lane that reads alarming forever and trains the override reflex that kills the guard. RELAYED from the driving desk cachyos-setup-b3, carried as theirs and NOT re-measured here: counting the uncited markers with grep -c returns 1 rather than 3, because law 16's marker WRAPS mid-phrase across the hard-wrapped prose, so a line-based count under-reads the very markers the predicate greps for. If the exemption form ends up counting markers it must not count them that way. NOT MEASURED AND DELIBERATELY NOT CLAIMED: nothing here establishes whether WRITER triggers should be wired at all, which is the round's question and not this entry's.

amended-evidence: 2026-09-19 RE-GROUNDED 2026-09-19 at the round desk. MEASURED (by the Begehung lane, verified at this desk against docs/begehung-findings-2026-09-19-r3.tsv row 3, its executed grep quoted there): lanes.evaluate_trigger has FOUR call sites at HEAD, one of them declaration.py:1624 inside read_moments, landed by lc-224; the booked three-sites/none-in-declaration claim is false at HEAD. DERIVED (from that measurement): the retire-until-something-evaluates repair is dead, since something now evaluates a predicate mode, for READERS. ## lc-238
grade: PARKED
requirement: NOTHING DETECTS A REPO LEFT BEHIND BY A SCHEMA BUMP, AND THE ROSTER THAT WOULD NAME THE POPULATION IS 90 PERCENT STALE. Only a schema ABOVE the floor is refused (declaration.py:485, schema_above_floor); BELOW the floor is silent, so a repo degrades rather than failing and no check anywhere says so. This is routed finding (g), whose live population turned out to be eight repos and twenty-six kinds rather than the single instance the finding described. The enumeration home is not missing: ~/.config/lifecycle/repos exists, is documented at CLAUDE.md:410, and lane list is GENERATED over it. It lists ONE repo of ten, not even this one. So the roster is the same silent-population defect one level up, and a detector keyed to the roster alone would inherit it.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/lanes.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_lanes.py
done-criterion: TWO findings, and the second is what stops the detector inheriting the defect it exists to catch. ONE: a repo whose declaration sits BELOW this build's SCHEMA_FLOOR is a FINDING naming the repo and both numbers, never silence. TWO: a declaration present on disk and ABSENT from the roster is a FINDING, so the roster is policed by a sweep instead of being another hand-maintained fact. RED-FIRST, and the positive control population already exists rather than being planted: the eight repos measured at schema 2 under floor 3 on 2026-09-19 must ALL be named by arm one today, and the nine of ten repos missing from the roster must be named by arm two today. CONTROL: the two repos at floor 3 must NOT be named by arm one, and a roster naming a repo that does carry a declaration must not be named by arm two, so neither arm fires on legitimate state. MUST-NOT-MOVE: it REPORTS and never migrates or edits another repo; a cross-repo write is a boundary act for a desk, not a checker's. MUST-NOT-MOVE 2: the sweep's reach is stated wherever its result is printed, because an absence claim from a rooted sweep is only as wide as its roots.
evidence: MEASURED 2026-09-19 at this desk, and the sweep carries a positive control (this repo's own declaration appears in its output): find over the home root at maxdepth 6 for .claude/lifecycle.json, excluding plugin caches and node_modules, returns 23 hits; setting aside 4 marketplace copies and 9 statiker run-arm clones under dev/local leaves TEN real working repos carrying a declaration. Of those, TWO are at schema 3 (lifecycle, dotfiles) and EIGHT at schema 2 (CachyOS-Setup, beat-the-books, begehung, daneel, dispatch-guards, skill-craft, statiker, claude-code-cache-fix). DEGRADED rather than merely stamped old, with a control: all 26 kinds across the eight lack the trigger stage lc-168 made required, while the two at floor 3 lack none. CONFIRMED AT THE EFFECT SITE by running the tool rather than reading JSON: lifecycle --repo <r> kind check prints FINDING [kind_stage_undeclared] ... undeclared: trigger for every kind in dispatch-guards and in CachyOS-Setup. RELAYED from the driving desk cachyos-setup-b3, verified independently there and carried as theirs: the same ten repos and the same 8/2 split, plus a fourth state this desk undersold, dispatch-guards' LEDGER.md carrying no schema head line at all, which is could-not-verify rather than behind. ROSTER READ AT THE ARTIFACT by this desk: ~/.config/lifecycle/repos holds one path, dotfiles, and its own header says an absent roster is BROKEN never an empty board. CORRECTION RECORDED AGAINST THIS DESK: I first reported that no roster existed, having searched for a VERB rather than for the config file lane list reads. The roster is not missing, it is stale, which is the sharper finding and is the driving desk's.

The repos are now named WITHOUT paths, which is the form this entry's own requirement and evidence already used and which the scan does not flag. ## lc-240
grade: PARKED
requirement: RETRIEVAL IS ALL-OR-NOTHING AND SHOULD BE TWO-STAGE, AND DECISIONS ARE INDEXED BY NOTHING. A session either ingests an artifact wholly at start or never reaches for it, so the environment's only lever today is loading MORE, which is the reader-stops-reading disease. The operator's shape instead: a lean always-on INDEX whose entry (a tool exists that relates to my task) triggers a LOOK, where the look supplies both the pattern and any recorded prior decision, the model's own relevance judgment does the matching, and the environment only keeps the index in view. THE GAP BEYOND THE INDEX ITSELF, and it is the part nothing here covers: DECISIONS INDEXED BY THE ARTIFACT THEY CONCERN. The ledger holds decisions chronologically and nothing surfaces the fact that an operator decision exists about a thing shaped like the task in hand. This is a GENERALIZATION rather than an invention: three local instances already exist (the runbook router table, kind list --digest, and the structure line lc-174 shipped), and the round decides the shape.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: UNKNOWN
done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending the round, deliberately: the round holds the design and this entry must not pre-empt it. What is FIXED as the shape the design answers to: an index entry is a DESCRIPTION rather than the content, its absence for a relevant artifact is the under-trigger failure, its presence for an irrelevant one is the over-trigger failure, and its divergence from the body it describes is the drift failure. The design names which of the three it detects and how, or says which it does not. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the environment keeps the index in view and never does the matching. The operator's own statement is that the model's relevance judgment does that, so a mechanism that decided relevance would be replacing the judgment this arc exists to feed rather than feeding it.
evidence: OPERATOR-STATED 2026-09-19, first-hand at the driving desk cachyos-setup-b3 and RELAYED here, carried as theirs and not re-measured at this desk. THE WORKED SPECIMEN, their account: the Marvel arc built tool one auto-starting with the game, a taste ruling stated once; tool two was then built NOT auto-starting. Their words, near-verbatim: made no sense, it never looked, it didn't even need my prior decision, if it had looked at the existing tool judgment would have been pretty clear. THEIR OWN NAMING OF THE MECHANISM, near-verbatim: like MCP or skills, they carry descriptions, not the whole corpus, and get loaded when relevant. DERIVED at the driving desk, three consequences. (1) AN INDEX ENTRY IS A SKILL DESCRIPTION, and description quality already has an eval discipline: skill-craft's Tier-1 triggering evals, blind router simulation with under and over-trigger grading, transfer directly to grading index entries. The round inherits an INSTRUMENT rather than only a pattern, which is rare for anything on its agenda. (2) THE FAILURE MODES TRANSFER TOO: under-trigger, which is the Marvel case since no entry existed; over-trigger, which is index noise and the reader-stops-reading disease; and description-body DRIFT, which is the label-over-body class. Lifecycle is better armed than the skill system on the third, because a kind carries a staleness stage and a skill listing does not. (3) THREE LOCAL INSTANCES ALREADY EXIST, so this is a generalization: the runbook router table, kind list --digest (lc-219), and the structure line lc-174 shipped and the driving desk wired into the session banner at dotfiles 9ef268d. MEASURED at this desk, the one thing verified here rather than relayed: that third instance is live, since session-scan.py:239 calls kind list --structure and renders its lines. DERIVED, placement: this is purpose.md's presence-as-selection made concrete, and it is the corpus's read-the-existing-instances rule made environmental rather than remembered. NOT MEASURED ANYWHERE: no count exists of how often a session failed to reach for an artifact it held, which is the same unbuilt measurement docs/answerable-not-felt.md names when it withdraws the retrieval grade.
blocked-by: decision has the design round closed, settling whether the index is a new kind, a widened digest, or a declaration field?
not-derivable: 2026-10-05 LEDGER.md:128 answers the round-closed question this entry was parked on and says in its own text that the SHAPE is still open: this answers the round, not the new-kind/digest/field question. docs/2026-09-19-round-decisions.md is the round body it points at; no decision line chooses among the three.
amend-reason: 2026-10-05 Blocker RE-TYPED (gap-sweep desk lifecycle-b5, 2026-10-05; named by the head pass at b97eb32 as a wait that is over). ## lc-248
grade: PARKED
requirement: THE DECLARATION IS THE ONE CARRIER THIS TOOL OWNS AND DOES NOT WRITE, so every kind ever added to it arrives by a hand edit over a whole-file rewrite. Law 8 makes the tool the only writer of the carriers it owns and the shape check catches a hand edit only where it BREAKS the shape; a re-serialised declaration parses perfectly while having silently dropped or reordered anything the writer did not hold in mind. The exposure is standing rather than hypothetical: it recurs at every kind addition, and the deletion-side read that would catch it is a discipline somebody has to remember. Record: found at the build desk 2026-09-19 while declaring the arc kinds, and raised by the round desk in the same exchange as the pit-of-success form of a read done by hand.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/cli.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_declaration.py
done-criterion: A kind is added BY VERB, and the verb emits the deletion-side summary the discipline asks a session to read by hand: top-level keys before and after, kind names before and after, and any pre-existing value whose content changed - printed at write time, so the lazy path is the correct path and nothing is owed to memory. RED-FIRST: a write that would drop a top-level key or an existing kind must REFUSE, proven on a declaration where the caller's payload omits one; CONTROL: an ordinary kind addition passes and prints a summary naming zero removals, so the guard is not simply 'refuse every write'. MUST-NOT-BUILD: nothing that makes the declaration tool-only - a human-edited declaration stays legal, because this repo's own declaration is read by people and a verb that claimed exclusive authorship would be asserting a property no check can hold. What is bought here is that the TOOL's own path stops being the unguarded one.
evidence: MEASURED at the build desk 2026-09-19: no verb in this package writes the declaration - a grep for writers of `.claude/lifecycle.json` returns only test fixtures (refusals.py's scratch repos), so the production path for adding a kind is a hand edit. MEASURED, this session's own instance: adding the two arc kinds re-serialised the whole file, 111 insertions and 80 deletions for two additions, and the deletion side was read AFTER the commit rather than before - it verified clean (top-level keys identical, no kind lost, no pre-existing value changed), which is exactly the outcome that makes the ordering easy to skip next time. RELAYED from the round desk 2026-09-19 and carried as theirs: they read the same diff stat independently and named the standing exposure of the writer, suggesting the summary-at-write-time form. DERIVED: a clean parse proves nothing about a whole-file write, because the result parses whether or not it kept what it replaced - which is why the instrument has to be the deletion side and why a mechanism beats a duty here.
blocked-by: decision freeze release - does the lc-161 after-measurement verdict, under the admission bar, release the new mechanisms parked by the lc-292 pass
not-derivable: 2026-09-25 Not derivable: the freeze (LEDGER decision 2026-09-24 freeze-and-refocus) holds new mechanisms until the lc-161 after-measurement reports, and the admission bar (LEDGER 2026-09-24) needs a pre-registered probe per candidate; no ledger line answers the release yet (searched LEDGER decisions for lc-161 after and freeze release); one answer re-releases every item parked on this exact question.
amend-reason: 2026-09-19 the evidence gains the measured instance the item was booked on a reasoned gap. A hand-rolled declaration write mangled 80 lines, shipped, and was undone by luck - and the repo's own add_lane docstring had already recorded that precise hazard. An item arguing for a verb is worth more with the damage the verb's absence caused than with the argument alone.
amended-evidence: 2026-09-19 MEASURED at this desk 2026-09-19: no verb in this package writes the declaration - a grep for writers of `.claude/lifecycle.json` returns only test fixtures - so the production path for adding a kind is a hand edit. MEASURED, AND THIS IS THE INSTANCE THAT PRICES THE ITEM: the hand edit that declared the arc kinds (95eea06) rewrote 80 LINES of real em-dashes into \\u2014 escapes, because it used json.dumps with the default ensure_ascii=True. It was pushed in that state and was reversed only by ACCIDENT, when a later commit happened to use ensure_ascii=False to match add_lane's contract; the restoration showed up as a 176-line diff on a one-kind change, which is what made anyone look. DERIVED, and it is the sharp part: `add_lane`'s own docstring ALREADY documents this exact failure - it records that the default rewrites 217 lines' worth of em-dash and section-sign into escapes and that a one-name registration would arrive buried in it. So the knowledge was in the repo, in the function nearest the act, and a desk hand-rolling the write still walked into it. That is the pit-of-success argument in one measurement: the correct predicate existed one import away and the nearest thing won. MEASURED, the earlier instance in the same item: the same hand edit was a whole-file write whose DELETION side was read after the commit rather than before. RELAYED from the round desk 2026-09-19 and carried as theirs: they named the standing exposure and suggested the summary-at-write-time form.
amend-reason: 2026-10-04 the blocker waited on the lc-161 after-measurement verdict, which no longer exists as one event: lc-161 took the per-arc form (LEDGER:173, LEDGER:175) and the release was ruled per mechanism (LEDGER:177). ## lc-249
grade: PARKED
requirement: DOORS AND GATES GRADE TRANSITIONAL STATES BY DIFFERENT READINGS, AND THE DISAGREEMENT SURFACES AS A REFUSAL ON LEGITIMATE WORK. A write door accepts a body and a later gate refuses the same body, or the reverse - so an author doing the right thing is stopped, which is the shape law 11 names as training the override reflex that kills a guard. THREE SPECIMENS FROM THIS ROUND, each cited with its incident. (a) THE PARK DOOR accepted `--not-derivable` beside an EVIDENCE blocker while the commit gate refused the same body - observed at the round desk cachyos-setup-43 on lc-52's re-park day, RELAYED here and carried as theirs. (b) THE RE-TYPE DOOR refused over PARKED plus `blocked-by NONE`, so the grade had to TRANSIT READY to reach a legal state - MEASURED at this desk during lc-199's close: the transit was the only path and the door had no reading for the intermediate body. (c) THE CLOSE-BLOCK GATE refused what the AMEND DOOR had accepted, until lc-241 P3a graded conditional-slot placement against the blocker IN FORCE rather than against the blocker as written - MEASURED at this desk, and P3a's fix is a SPECIMEN of the class rather than its repair. WHAT MAKES IT ONE CLASS rather than three bugs: in all three the two readers are correct about different moments. A door grades the body it is HANDED; a gate grades the body as it will BE. Between them sits a transitional state that neither reader's predicate was written for, and nothing in either message says the other reader exists. Record: this round's build wave, 2026-09-19.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: UNKNOWN
done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending the blocker's answer, and the entry must not pre-empt it: the two named repairs are a SINGLE GRADING PATH both doors and gates call, or a DECLARED PER-DOOR EXEMPTION at each of the three sites. MUST-NOT-BUILD, and it is the whole of law 11 here: NO SOFTENED PREDICATE anywhere. A door that stops refusing the transitional body by widening its own test has not reconciled the two readings, it has deleted one of them - and the gate that still refuses will then fire on a body the door promised was fine, which is this class one turn later and harder to see. lc-241 P3a's in-force fix STANDS and is specimen (c), never the repair: it corrected ONE site's reading and is exactly the per-site move whose repeatability across the other two is the question.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk 2026-09-19, specimens (b) and (c), both reproduced during this round's own build wave and each recorded in its item's close block: lc-199's re-type transit through READY, and lc-241 P3a's placement grading against the blocker in force. RELAYED from the round desk cachyos-setup-43 and carried as theirs, specimen (a): the park door accepting `--not-derivable` beside an evidence blocker where the commit gate refused the same body, observed on lc-52's re-park day - NOT re-measured here, and the discriminating detail (which door and which gate, at what commit) is theirs to supply if the repair turns on it. DERIVED, the class statement: three instances sharing one shape is a pattern and not a proof, and the shape is that a door grades what it is handed while a gate grades what will be - so the population this entry asserts is exactly three, with no claim that a sweep would not find more. NOT MEASURED ANYWHERE: no sweep of the package's doors against its gates has been run, so how many such pairs exist is unknown and is the first thing the repair design should establish. JOIN DISPOSED as `new` against lc-248, which the join flagged on 8 shared requirement tokens (2026-09-19, build, desk, rather, record, round, shape, write) - every one a date or a word this round's prose carries everywhere, which is the shared-boilerplate match the join's own design warns reads as a duplicate. Read at the artifact rather than off the score: lc-248 asks for a VERB that writes the declaration so a hand edit stops being the production path; this asks whether two CORRECT readers of one body can be reconciled. Different subjects, no shared write-set path, and neither's done-criterion advances the other.
blocked-by: decision do the three specimens share ONE repair - a single grading path both doors and gates call - or is each a declared per-door exemption?
not-derivable: 2026-09-19 The record does not answer it and I looked: no ledger decision line names door-versus-gate grading, no audit in docs/audits/ covers it, and the declaration carries no door/gate contract to read a precedent off. The three specimens support BOTH repairs equally, which is what makes this a design choice rather than a measurement - a single shared path buys consistency and costs a refactor across every door and gate; a per-door exemption buys locality and costs a rule that must be re-applied correctly at each new site. Nothing in the record ranks those.
amend-reason: 2026-09-19 the park door's own write replaced the admission-time statement with a shorter one that asserts the absence WITHOUT naming the search that established it - which is the bare-absence shape lc-169's slot exists to refuse. Restored with each search named. Noted rather than silently fixed: a door overwriting a conditional slot it also demands is a SPECIMEN of this very entry's class, found while booking it.
amended-not-derivable: 2026-09-19 The record does not answer it and these are the searches that did not find it, named because an absence claim is worth exactly its instrument's reach: LEDGER.md grepped for each specimen's own terms (park door, re-type, close-block, not-derivable, in force) - no decision line names door-versus-gate grading; docs/audits/ listed and none covers doors or gates; the declaration carries no door/gate contract to read a precedent off. The three specimens are the entire evidence base and they support BOTH repairs equally, which is what makes this a design choice rather than a measurement: a single shared path buys consistency and costs a refactor across every door and gate; a per-door exemption buys locality and costs a rule that must be re-applied correctly at each new site. Nothing in the record ranks those, and the ranking is a judgment about what this package should be.
amend-reason: 2026-09-19 FOURTH SPECIMEN RULED IN by the round desk cachyos-setup-43, 2026-09-19. The count was asserted in the requirement and a requirement is not a peer's to inflate silently, so it was surfaced rather than added; this amendment is that ruling executed. The specimen widens the class by one axis the first three did not reach - it is a DOOR-versus-DOOR disagreement over one slot's required content, not door-versus-gate - and it is the only member whose failure is SILENT, which is why the ruling calls it the sharpest. CodeBolt drift detection: a sidecar loads the original task, the initial plan and recent event-log phases; a separate model grades on_track/drifting/off_track with graded reactions (reminder; replan or human pause); its docs state the acting agent is a poor judge of its own drift (desk-verified page). FAA plan-continuation bias: periodic conformance checks with the replan-triggering evidence declared in advance (lane-verified). ContinuityBench: a drift test set needs hard negatives, legitimate pivots that look like drift (lane-verified). Cautions: ReflAct (arXiv 2505.15182) is per-action self-reflection, excluded here by purpose.md's intrinsic-self-correction evidence; a model judge over prose tops out near AUROC 0.65, so an outside grader reads STATE (goal line, plan, fire log), never narrative.

## lc-329
grade: STANDBY
requirement: NOTHING FLAGS A LIVE ITEM WHOSE GROUND HAS MOVED SINCE IT WAS BOOKED. The items kind declares staleness as a record pointer that no longer resolves, and no verb reads whether the files in an item's write-set changed after its booking. On 2026-10-07 the drain waves found items already repaired or overtaken (lc-233 and lc-122 dropped, several NOT-REPRODUCED) only because each lane reproduced before building. Operator question, first-hand 2026-10-07: do governed repos judge backlog items as they pile up, since they may be superseded or need adjusting. Record: docs/directives/2026-10-07-drain-wave-e.md
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/cli.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: A reading verb lists, per live item, the commits that touched a file of its write-set after the item's booking commit or its newest amendment, with the count; an item with none says so; an item whose write-set is UNKNOWN, a venue, or a path git does not track is listed as NOT GRADED with the reason, never as unchanged. The list FINDS and moves nothing: no grade changes and no drop (law 10). Red-first: in a fixture repo an item whose write-set file is committed after its booking is listed and a sibling whose file is untouched is not. Before FIXED the line count is measured over every rostered repo and reported.
evidence: MEASURED 2026-10-07 at the drain desk: a search for stale, overtaken, aged over items.py and verbs.py finds no per-item check, and the items kind in .claude/lifecycle.json declares staleness as change-coupling on a record pointer. DERIVED: the flow ratio reads the trend of the whole carrier and says nothing about one item.
blocked-by: NONE

grade: STANDBY

On 2026-10-07 that comparison caught lc-62 (about 300 new lines on one repo), lc-194 (statiker ready from exit 0 to 2) and lc-325 (66 bookings refused), and its absence is how lc-279 shipped the finding lc-315 records (9 of 9 declarations failing). It writes nothing in any repo, asserted by a status read before and after. Red-first: a planted new finding in the new checkout shows as a change over a fixture roster, and two identical checkouts show none. The Verify section of CLAUDE.md names it for changes that add or widen a finding.

The output states how many arrangements ran and on how many workers.

grade: STANDBY

The commit gate accepts the amend's own commit.
evidence: MEASURED 2026-10-07 at the drain desk on the live carrier with --no-commit: item amend lc-154 --blocked-by NONE removed one line (the base not-derivable) and added two; item check then printed FINDING item_shape, block lc-154, amended-not-derivable amends a slot the block does not carry. The carrier was restored from the committed copy and reads CLEAN again.

## lc-334
grade: STANDBY
requirement: THE EMIT-SITE SCAN READS DOCSTRINGS AS EMIT SITES. roster.emit_sites matches the bracketed row idiom anywhere in a source file, so prose inside a docstring counts: for trigger_broken it returns two lines that sit inside cmd_item_statusline's docstring. A row whose real emit sites were deleted would still read as emitted, a docstring naming an unregistered row would fire a false finding, and the prover's per-row site counts are inflated. Record: docs/directives/2026-10-07-drain-wave-e.md
goal: every-refusal-red-first
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/roster.py,test/test_roster.py
done-criterion: The scan counts a bracketed row name only where it is code that can execute: a name inside a docstring or a comment is not an emit site. Red-first, both directions: a planted module whose ONLY mention of a registered row is in a docstring makes that row read as not emitted where the real sites are removed; and a docstring naming an unregistered row raises no emit_site_unregistered. Control: every row emitted today is still counted, and the count per row is printed before and after so a dropped real site shows.
evidence: RELAYED from wave E lane E4 2026-10-07 at 83eaa73, not re-run at the desk: emit_sites() for trigger_broken returns lanes.py:1031, lanes.py:1046, verbs.py:2570, verbs.py:2576, verbs.py:2891, and 2570 and 2576 are prose inside a docstring.
blocked-by: NONE

The next carrier verb then refuses at entry on a dirty carrier, and a co-writer's pathspec commit can carry the migration out under its own message. Record: docs/directives/2026-10-07-drain-wave-e.md
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/cli.py,test/test_migrate.py
done-criterion: Every migrate run that wrote a tracked file ends by naming the files written and either commits them by pathspec or prints NOT COMMITTED with the commit command, through the one body lc-41 added (desk.say_not_committed); a dry run and a run that wrote nothing print neither. migrate leaves the UNCLAIMED class in the lc-41 enumeration test. Red-first: an applying run over a scratch repo prints no such line today.
evidence: RELAYED from wave E lane E9 2026-10-07: migrate left UNCLAIMED in its commit-or-say enumeration because migrate.py and cli.py were outside its write set. MEASURED at the desk the same day: three --schema-from --apply runs (begehung, daneel, claude-code-cache-fix) each left four modified files and printed only the APPLIED line. Also observed there: the apply re-serializes the declaration, turning escaped dashes and section signs into literal characters, lines the plan never listed.
blocked-by: NONE

## lc-337
grade: STANDBY
requirement: ONE LEAK-SCAN BITE FAILS WHEN XDG_STATE_HOME POINTS AT A SCRATCH PATH. The node battery's foreign-path arm for the known XDG roots (env default) reads the environment, so a caller that isolates state for a probe, as every brief here tells lanes to do, gets 61 pass and 1 fail where the same tree gives 62 pass. A battery whose verdict depends on the caller's isolation trains readers to discount its red. Record: docs/directives/2026-10-07-drain-wave-e.md
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks

done-criterion: node --test test/absence-scan.test.mjs gives the same counts with XDG_STATE_HOME unset, set to its default, and set to a scratch directory; the arm pins the root it means inside the test and never reads the caller's value. Red-first: the scratch setting fails one bite today. tools/verify-claude-md.py then stops special-casing node commands.
evidence: RELAYED from wave E lane E5 2026-10-07, not re-run at the desk: with XDG_STATE_HOME on scratch the bite named foreign-path: a path under each known XDG root (env default) fails, 61 pass 1 fail against 62 pass; the lane limited its scratch override to python commands for that reason.
blocked-by: NONE

## lc-338
grade: STANDBY
requirement: A LIFECYCLE VERB KILLED AT ITS CALLER'S TIME BUDGET LEAVES ITS git CHILD RUNNING. The CLI starts git as a plain child; a caller that kills the verb's process at a bound (the dotfiles session-start hook does) kills the verb and not the grandchild, so a git that never answers stays behind, one per call. Nothing in the CLI bounds its own git calls or puts them in a group the caller can kill. Record: docs/directives/2026-10-07-drain-wave-e.md
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/retire.py,test/test_verbs.py
done-criterion: DECIDED which side owns it and built there: either every git call the CLI makes carries a timeout and a kill of its own process group, answering COULD NOT VERIFY with the reason when git did not answer, or the CLI documents in --help that a caller bounding it must kill by group and the dotfiles hook does. Red-first with a git stub that never answers first on PATH: after the verb is cut off no stub process started by it remains.
evidence: RELAYED from peer session dotfiles-2b 2026-10-07, lane-reported there and not re-measured at either desk: with a git stub (exec sleep 100) first on PATH, the dotfiles session-start hook cut the lifecycle CLI off at its bound and two orphaned stub processes remained, started by the CLI; they were retired by PID.
blocked-by: NONE
